The second may not rally, turn the line, strike anybody down, be wounded, see the day end, look for the other man, be looked at by him, think about his marriage in any warm way, or use the words *shame*, *pride* or *honour* | 2 templates, base 50, 80 |
| the thing he does not have any more | The mod had mentioned dragonriders exactly twice and both times in a comment: the note at the top of `bc_knight_phase_events.txt` records that AGOT's own combat phase events include a dragonrider entry at `base = 100000` gated behind `is_current_dragonrider_warfare`, and therefore does not crowd this pool. That was the whole of the engagement — **no gate anywhere read a dragon trait**. Both new gates are the photographic negative of that AGOT event: it fires for a man who has one on this field, these fire for a man who used to have one and is standing in the mud like everybody else. `dragonrider` is never read; the two traits read are the two AGOT swaps it for, and **both are required and neither is sufficient** — in `agot_dragon.0018` a rider whose dragon has just died loses `dragonrider` and takes `dragonwidowed` if `scope:dragonwidowed` exists and `dragonless_dragonrider` otherwise, while `agot_untame_dragon` hands out `dragonless_dragonrider` again for a beast that is still alive and simply no longer his. The mod does not care which of the three routes it was, only that the man could once do a thing he cannot do today. **Neither gate reads `agot_dragon`, the relation, and that is deliberate**: it is self-corresponding, so it is held by the dragon as well as the rider and a gate built on it would be true of the beast. The traits are only ever on people. The second gate carries the first as an explicit negation, which also settles the one ambiguous case — `any_side_knight` hands back root himself if root is knighted on this side, so without it a man would satisfy the second gate by being the first. Having been the rider is the larger fact and wins the tie. The commander half is `adapted`/`down` and hands out nothing, which is the point: nothing about the army changed when he lost the thing, only the size of what he is allowed to attempt. The problem in front of him is not a hard problem, and that is what makes the afternoon what it is — he looked at the far side's right before midday and knew in the flat immediate way a man knows a familiar road exactly what wanted doing, and then had to put all of it down, because the doing of it required something not available to him, and go looking for the next best method, which is slower and costs men and is not certain. It takes an hour; in that hour the far side moves and the shape he was answering stops existing, so he begins again, and **the second answer is worse than the first was**, and it also takes an hour. He is not being outthought. He is being asked to do everything by a longer route than the one he spent twenty years learning to trust. The knight half is `wild`/`up`, a rung and `bc_spent_horse` in the same breath, which is what a whole rank ends up riding like when the pace at the front of it is being set by a man comparing it to something no horse has ever done. Within a hundred paces the rank is no longer a rank, because one man in it is riding as though the animal underneath him has personally offended him by existing — not bravery, and it does not look like bravery, but **impatience of a very specific and unusual kind**, the impatience of somebody measuring this not against other charges but against some other standard entirely that nobody around him has access to, in which a hundred paces is not a distance worth counting. Root gives his horse more than he intended to give it, twice, because the alternative is to be visibly behind, and the second time he feels the animal start to change underneath him. They hit, and it works, and it works partly because they arrived while the far side was still deciding. Neither desc may use the words *dragon*, *wing*, *fly*, *flew*, *flight*, *fire*, *burn*, *scale*, *claw*, *beast*, *mount* or *sky*, or give the animal a name, colour, size or sex; the engine has already established what he used to be and the prose does not need to say it out loud to be understood. Neither may stage the loss — no death scene, no last flight, no falling, no account of how it went, because AGOT reaches the same two traits by three different routes and the mod does not distinguish them. Neither may give him any of it back in a memory longer than a clause, a dream, an omen, a shadow crossing the field, or anybody else mistaking something for it. The first may not turn the loss around, win anything, be quoted giving an order, be considered great or ruined by anybody, say a word about what he used to be, be wounded, consider his own death, or be comforted or noticed at all. The second may not be about the other man's feelings, have the two speak or acknowledge each other, have the other man do anything remarkable with a weapon, kill anybody, wound root, end the day, or have root pity him, envy him or be told anything about him by a third person — root only ever has the evidence of the pace | 2 templates, base 50, 80 |
| the men who are standing here as terms | The mod had **never read the hostage system**. `is_hostage`, `is_hostage_of` and `is_hostage_from` were all at zero occurrences, and AGOT uses the system — it appears in its activities, its interactions and its casus belli files — so these are not gates on an empty table. **The three triggers are not interchangeable and the dump is explicit about it**: `is_hostage_of` asks whether the target is the warden currently holding him, `is_hostage_from` asks whether the target is the original liege who handed him over, and `is_hostage` alone asks neither. The gates use one each of the first two, and neither uses the third, because a bare `is_hostage` gate would be a superset of both and could not be told apart from them. **The first is deliberately the original liege and not the warden**: a hostage lives in his warden's court, so the army he stands in is almost always the warden's, and a gate asking whether the warden commands the far side would describe a situation the game very rarely produces — asking whether the man who gave him up commands it describes the one it produces all the time, which is the two parties going to war over precisely the thing he was handed across to prevent. The second is the same arrangement from the warden's end and carries the first as an explicit negation, since being held is no bar to holding somebody; being the terms yourself is the larger fact and wins the tie. The commander half is `measured`/`up` and hands out nothing, which is right — what he notices does not change how anything performs, it changes what he will have to decide about afterwards. He sees it from the rise among a hundred other things and takes a moment longer than he should to understand it: there is a section of his line that is not giving, and the reason is one man who has decided, apparently some time ago and without telling anybody, **that today is the day he stops being an item and starts being a soldier**. He is doing it badly and expensively and with enormous conviction, and he is also doing it, and the line either side has stopped looking for permission to move back because the man in the middle of it plainly has not considered the possibility. The commander watches the day being won in that one place by an arrangement that was never meant to fight, and finds he is already thinking about what he will do with it in the spring, when it stops being a good afternoon and goes back to being a piece of paper with a life attached. The knight half is `bloodied`/`down` and gets `bc_bloodied`, nothing clever and no rally. There is a banner on the far side he has known the look of since he was small, and it is not a comfort and it is not an enemy either, and the afternoon is spent in the space between those two things — everything he was ever told about who he is came from under it, and everything he has actually done was done a long way from it, on somebody else's ground, in a house that was scrupulously kind to him **in the specific way you are kind to something valuable**. So the line goes back and he goes back with it, and he is not fighting well because there is nothing to fight well for: whoever wins this, the thing he is does not change, and he understood that before the first hour was out. What he does instead is refuse to be the reason it goes any faster, and takes more than his share of what comes at him, because taking it is the only part of today that is his to decide. Neither desc may name the other man or give his house, title, nickname or sex, or use the words *hostage*, *ward*, *warden*, *ransom*, *surety* or *guarantee*. Neither may stage the handover, the ride, the parting or any scene older than a clause, state the terms, name what was promised, or say what happens to him if the promise breaks — the threat is the structure of both pages and neither page says it. Neither may have the two men meet, speak, look at each other or be pointed out to each other, and nobody else on the field may mention the arrangement out loud. The first may not decide anything, give an order that is quoted, act on what he notices, warn or send anybody away, resolve into a plan, feel guilty, feel fond, or say a word to the man in question. The second may not kill anybody, be wounded, rally, turn the line, end the day, try to reach the man across the field, consider changing sides, think about home, be noticed, or use the words *father*, *son*, *boy* or *child* | 2 templates, base 50, 80 |
| the reputations that are not his | The mod had mentioned accolades once, in a comment, to borrow their spelling — the note beside `bc_his_own_people_have_a_ledger_trigger` cites `04_ep2_eminent_attributes.txt` for writing the three kinslayer traits out longhand — and that was the entirety of it. `is_accolade_successor` and `any_acclaimed_knight` were both at zero occurrences, and the accolade files are present under this load order, so neither gate is aimed at an empty table. **These two are not a pair in the usual sense and are deliberately not written as one.** Every recent batch has been one arrangement seen from both ends, with the second carrying the first as an explicit negation; these are two different facts about fame that happen to come out of the same system, they are aimed at different pools — the first at knights, the second at commanders — and those pools roll separately, so there is nothing for a negation to protect. Writing one in anyway would have cost each gate its best case: a man who is next in line for a name is exactly the sort of man a commander with no famous knights of his own would want. **The first is a state, not an achievement**, and that is the whole moment — the engine's successor is not a man who has an accolade, he is a man the engine has written down as the one who gets it when somebody else is finished with it, and there is no glory on him today and there may never be. The second is a comparison across the field, the only gate in the file that asks the same question of both commanders and keeps the answer only when it comes back lopsided; the near miss worth naming is `bc_side_is_outnumbered_trigger`, which compares what the two armies are made of on the day, while this compares what the two households have been able to keep. The commander half is `adapted`/`down` and hands out nothing, which is the argument: the far side's famous men are not why he is losing, and knowing that does not help. It occurs to him somewhere in the wreck of the second attempt at the left that everything coming at him has already been talked about somewhere he was not — not bitterly, and not for long, simply a fact about the field the way the slope is a fact. The men over there have been paid for in gifts and in patience and in years of somebody keeping them fed and pleased and near at hand; the men on this side have been paid for too, in coin, at the going rate, and the difference between those two arrangements is standing in front of him doing what it was always going to do. **He is not sure the far side is fighting better** — that is the strange part, and he goes back to it twice while the line comes apart. What he can find is that his own people are waiting in a way they would not be waiting if the ground were reversed, and there is nobody in his household this year whose going first would settle anything. The knight half is `wild`/`down` and gets `bc_spent_horse`, the correct price for a man riding at the version of himself that exists on a piece of parchment in somebody else's chamber. The day has gone wrong in the ordinary way, without a moment in it anybody will remember, and that is the thing he cannot stand about it: **a defeat with nothing in it**. He rides at the part of the far line furthest from anything useful, because it is the part where a man can be seen, three times, and nothing comes of it, and by the third pass his horse is doing something with its head that he has felt before and has never once ignored, and he ignores it. Neither desc may name anybody living or dead, or use the words *accolade*, *acclaimed*, *glory*, *renown*, *legend* or *famous*; both pages are about reputation and neither is allowed the vocabulary of it. Neither may quote a song, a singer, a herald, a boast or a rumour, or have anybody on the field say anything about anybody's name out loud — reputation in both arrives here as weather. The first may not count the far side's men, describe one of them, have one of them do anything, lose to one in particular, turn the day around, be quoted giving an order, be wounded, be comforted, or decide that the far side's names are deserved or undeserved; the page is not allowed an opinion, only an observation. The second may not succeed — no kill of consequence, not seen by the person whose opinion he is riding for, not praised, not stopped, not wounded in a way that ends anything — and may not name what he is inheriting or from whom, think about the other man's death, admit to himself what he is doing, or use the words *prove*, *worthy* or *deserve* | 2 templates, base 50, 80 |
| how many people are left with that name | The mod reads houses and had **never counted one**. `bc_foe_is_house_trigger` and its neighbours ask whether two men share a house; nothing anywhere asked how many people are in it, and `any_dynasty_member` and `dynasty_prestige_level` were both at zero occurrences. **The count is two and the threshold is borrowed, not invented** — vanilla's own `00_relation_triggers.txt` uses `any_dynasty_member = { count <= 2 }` twice for the same idea, and two is the number that makes the gate mean what its name says: him, and at most one other person anywhere in the world. Three would have caught every minor house on the map. `is_alive = yes` is inside the count on purpose: the iterator is believed to list living members only, and if that is right the filter costs nothing, but if it is ever wrong the filter is the difference between "nearly the last of his house" and "a house that has not existed for two hundred years", which is not a difference this mod can afford to leave to belief. `dynasty` is an optional link and takes the standing guard — this setting is full of lowborn hedge knights and sellswords — and the second gate guards it twice, once on the far commander and once on that man's dynasty. The second carries the first as an explicit negation, since two nearly-extinct houses meeting on one field is most of what the later part of a long war looks like; being the last one yourself is the larger fact and wins the tie. Neither is `bc_foe_is_house_trigger`, which asks whether two men share a name rather than how much of a name is left, and neither is `bc_his_own_people_have_a_ledger_trigger`, which counts what a man has done to his own vassals — a thing he chose — while this counts something done to him by thirty years of other people's wars. The commander half is `entrenched`/`down`, another of the three pairs plan 1 called unreachable, and gets `bc_shieldwall` precisely because **the thing being held is not ground**. It arrives at him sideways, the way the important things do, while he is watching the second rank shuffle to fill a gap that is going to open again in a moment — not a thought about dying, he has had those and they are ordinary and they do not feel like this, but a much flatter observation, almost clerical: that whatever this afternoon does to him it is also doing to a quantity, and **the quantity is now small enough to be held in the head without effort**, which was not true when he was young and is the whole of what has changed. He does not do anything about it. What it changes is the temperature of the standing — he stops thinking about the battle in terms of ground and starts thinking about it in terms of hours, and goes very calm, and the line, which has no idea why, finds that the orders coming down it have got shorter and stopped containing any doubt. The knight half is `ordered`/`up` and hands out `bc_pursuit`, which is exactly what it is: **the part of a battle that is no longer about the battle**. The fighting stopped about a mile back; what is happening now has the shape of fighting and the pace of work, and root is in it with perhaps forty others strung out across ground that is going soft, following a thing that has stopped being a formation and become a direction. He is aware, in the incurious way you are aware of the weather, that this is the shorter kind of afternoon — not the sort that ends when one army has had enough, but the sort that ends because there stops being anything on the other side of the question — and there is no order to that effect and nobody has said it, it is simply understood along the whole ragged length of the pursuit, and it makes everybody slightly quieter and slightly more careful with their horses. Neither desc may name a house, a person, a seat, a sigil or a motto, or use the words *extinct*, *line*, *blood*, *heir* or *last* of a person. Neither may list the dead, say how any of them died, give a war, a year or a place for it, or have anybody remember anybody — the subtractions all happened somewhere else. Neither may mention children, marriages, pregnancies, betrothals or any arrangement for continuing anything; both pages are about arithmetic that has already been done. The first may not rally, turn the line, be wounded, consider his own death directly, say anything to anybody, have the men around him know what he is thinking, be comforted, or end the day. The second may not reach the man, kill him, capture him, see him close up, hear him speak, feel pity, feel triumph, decide anything about it, or use the words *hunt*, *quarry* or *finish* | 2 templates, base 50, 80 |
| the shape of the rest of his wars | The mod asks who leads *this* war and has **never asked what else he is in**. `bc_this_is_his_own_war_trigger` reads `is_war_leader = root` off the war behind this battle; that is one war. `is_at_war_as_attacker` and `is_at_war_as_defender` were both at zero occurrences, and they are not about this war at all — they are the shape of a man's whole ledger of wars on the day he happens to be standing here. **The dump's caveat is the entire reason these are rare enough to be gates**: both are documented "Does not consider your lieges' wars", so a knight riding in his lord's host with no war of his own answers no to both, and so does every landless man on the field. What is left is the much smaller set of people who are personally, in their own name, at war — and then the gates ask what shape that is. The first is **deliberately an exclusive reading** and is not just `is_at_war_as_attacker`: a bare attacker gate would be true of half the map in any decade with a war in it, and would also be true of every man the second gate is about, making the second a strict subset. Spelling the negation in gives the first a meaning a bare one does not have — every war with his name on it today is one he chose to open. The second needs no negation of the first, because the first already excludes it by its own second row; they are disjoint in both directions, which is unusual here and is written into the trigger header so that nobody later tidies the `NOT` away and silently makes the pair overlap. Neither takes a guard: both are plain character triggers on root and neither reads a link. The commander half is `ordered`/`down`, the third of plan 1's unreachable pairs and one of the few moments that **deliberately hands out nothing** — `bc_pursuit` is for going forward and there is no version of this afternoon where he goes forward. The line is going backwards in good order, which is the only creditable thing about it, and he is running that retreat with a part of himself while the rest does sums about somewhere he is not: every man he puts into the gap on the right is a man not standing in a different gap, every hour this takes is an hour off a reckoning he is not present for. He has been carrying the pair of them since the thaw and has got tolerably good at it, the way a man gets good at something heavy — not by finding it lighter but by learning precisely where to put his hands and then not moving them. What he has never got good at is being shouted at by one half while holding the other, and each time the near business lets go he has to find his place in the far one again from the top. So he gives today the smallest correct answer there is: keep the shape, give the ground away evenly, put nothing into this that he will want in six weeks. `stewardship >= 12` and diligent/lazy, because running two wars out of one head while one of them is in front of you shouting is an administrative problem that happens to be taking place in a field. The knight half is `entrenched`/`up` and takes `bc_shieldwall` as the argument rather than the decoration: **the day is going well, which is precisely the danger**, because a day that is going well is a day when everybody around you wants it to go well faster. He has quarrels of his own and every one of them he opened — nobody has ever come at him, he has always gone — and today is a field he was asked onto, over a disagreement between two other men that will be settled in a room he will never see. He can feel the rank wanting to move, the way a horse tells you it has decided something before it does it, and what he does instead is get shorter and heavier: shoulders down, shields in, the two on his left told twice to stop leaning out to look. Forward eats horses and wind and shins, and every one of those is a thing he will need in the spring on ground that will be his own argument. `stewardship >= 12` again, and ambitious/content — fighting economically in somebody else's quarrel, because every hour and every man spent here is an hour and a man not spent on your own, is a decision of the purse worn as armour. Neither desc may name the other war, its enemy, its cause, its place or its year, or say how many wars there are; neither may use a messenger, a rider coming in, a letter, a dispatch or any news arriving, so nothing reaches either man from outside this field; and neither may use the words *campaign*, *front*, *strategy*, *theatre* or *invasion* or consult a map, the title of the first being allowed its figure of speech where the page is not. The first may not resolve into a decision, withdraw, break off, send anybody anywhere, rally, turn the line, end the day, say the word *enough*, be advised by anybody, or blame one person for anything. The second may not be about greed in words, price anything in coin, use *cost*, *profit*, *cheap* or *spend* of money, resent the man he is fighting for, charge, kill anybody, be praised, or end the day | 2 templates, base 50, 80 |
| what his years are actually going into | The mod reads what a man was **taught** and had never once read what he is spending his years on **now**: `has_focus` was at zero occurrences across the whole mod. An education trait is a fact about a boy; a lifestyle focus is a fact about what the man has chosen to put his attention into this decade, and it can be changed, and the two are not the same sentence. The near misses are `bc_he_was_raised_for_books_trigger` and `bc_he_was_raised_for_the_dark_trigger`, which wrap vanilla's education wrappers and ask where a man **started** — neither pair is a subset of the other in either direction, since a lord educated in arms can spend his forties on books and a maester-taught boy can spend his forties on nothing of the kind. The other near miss is `has_lifestyle`, already read as `martial_lifestyle` by `bc_he_was_pointed_at_war_and_left_trigger` and its partner; both new gates are disjoint from it by construction, because a learning focus and an intrigue focus each put a man in a lifestyle that is not the martial one. **The pair is disjoint without a negation, which is the exception here and is written into the header as a decision rather than an oversight**: a character holds exactly one focus and `set_focus` replaces rather than adds, so a `NOT` would be dead script implying a worry the engine already settles. The adventurer and pirate variants are deliberately left out — vanilla's `learning_adventurer_focus` and `intrigue_adventurer_focus`, and AGOT's `learning_scholarship_landless_pirate_focus` at `common/focuses/00_lifestyle_focuses.txt:1061` — because they belong to landless governments and describe a different life, and folding them in would widen both gates in exchange for a page that would then be wrong about half the men it fired for. The six keys are listed rather than grouped, there being no group key for a lifestyle's focuses, and all six are confirmed present in AGOT's own override. The commander half is `adapted`/`up` and hands out nothing, which is correct, because what changed was the reading and not the army. He has never thought of himself as a soldier and stopped apologising for that some while ago; what he has instead is an unglamorous slow habit of assuming that whatever is in front of him has rules in it he has not found yet, and of continuing to look some time after other men have decided they understand. He watches the far side's second attempt the way he would work at a passage he means to go through four times — not, at first, for what it means, but for what it does twice — and it does two things twice. The third attempt is still forming when he starts moving men who do not know why, and the fourth goes into a stretch of ground he has quietly arranged to be worse than it looks. **There is nothing dazzling anywhere in it**; by the middle of the afternoon he simply has more of the field in his head than the man opposite has, and that is all the difference there is. `learning >= 12` and patient/impatient. The knight half is `bloodied`/`down` and the modifier is the argument: the only tools he has left are the ones he has practised least. Everything he is good at wants two conditions a hedge cannot supply — time, and somebody willing to be alone with him — and eleven years of getting very accurate about how a thing is made to happen from the far side of a courtyard, without the person who made it happen ever being the person seen, is worth nothing in a rank folding from the right at the pace of a man walking. He has a sword because everybody has a sword, and he knows the four things you are taught with it the way you know words you have not said aloud since you were small: in sequence, without any sense the sequence is going to help. The first man to reach him he manages; the second he manages worse and takes something across the forearm for it, high up, where the mail stops being generous, and **the shock is less about the pain than about how ordinary the exchange was**. `intrigue >= 12` and paranoid/brave, because a man whose whole practice is arranging for things to happen without him being in the room has an unusually bad afternoon when the room is a field. Neither desc may stage a memory — no boyhood, no teacher, no hall, no courtyard scene, nobody remembered by name or office — and neither may name a book, a text, a title, a language, a maester, a septon, a letter or a scroll, or have anything written down. The first may not be clever at anybody: no out-thinking a named man, no being pleased with himself, no being admired, no explaining anything, no *genius*, *brilliant* or *scholar*, no rallying, no plan a reader could write down, and no ending the day. The second may not plot — no poison, no bribe, no arrangement made on the field, no accomplice, no name of anybody he has ever dealt with — and may not run, be captured, be rescued, kill anybody, call himself a coward, or end the day | 2 templates, base 50, 80 |
| who there is above him | The mod has read rank, tenure and weight and had **never read independence**. `highest_held_title_tier` and `is_landed` carry the station batch, `is_powerful_vassal` carries the weight batch, and all three describe a man's position *inside* a realm; `is_independent_ruler` was at zero occurrences and asks the one question none of them asks. The near miss is `bc_facing_a_crown_trigger`, which reads `highest_held_title_tier >= tier_kingdom` on the far commander — rank and independence are not the same fact in either direction, since a vassal king holds tier_kingdom and answers to an emperor while a merchant prince or a pirate lord answers to nobody at all holding nothing so grand. The other is `bc_this_is_his_own_war_trigger`, which asks who opened the war: a vassal can be war leader of his own rebellion while having a liege in the world, and independence is about the map rather than about today. **`is_landless_ruler` was considered as the second gate and rejected**: it is documented "holds any title, but no on-map land", which makes it a strict subset of the shipped `is_landed = no` at `bc_commander_phase_events.txt:1928`, and a gate contained by a gate the mod already fires on is not a new question — so the pairing is by direction instead, the second carrying the exact negation of the first. A field where both commanders answer to nobody produces the first and never the second, which is the correct precedence: being the man with nobody above him is the larger fact about an afternoon than noticing it about somebody else. The first is a commander-pool gate in practice, since a knight in somebody's line is a courtier or a vassal and answers no almost always; the second works from either pool, its subject being the man opposite and its only condition on root a negative one. The commander half is `adapted`/`down` and hands out nothing, because what he has worked out is not a manoeuvre. The thought turns up while the left is being pressed in for the second time, and it is not fear and it is not new — he has always known there is nobody over him, and it has been the quiet pleasure of his whole adult life. **What has changed is that the same sentence has stopped being a boast and become a description of his afternoon.** There is nobody whose business this also is, nobody with a larger interest who might be expected to spend men on it, no hand that will reach in from outside the hedge and alter the sum. So he alters what is underneath the orders, which do not change and would not change if help were coming: he stops making the class of decision that is worth making if somebody arrives on Thursday, and starts making the other kind, the ones you make when the whole of the future available to you is the next two hours and the road behind. The men see none of it; to them he has got quieter and begun answering in one syllable. `diplomacy >= 12` and stubborn/craven. The knight half is `wild`/`up`, which grants the rung and costs the horses in the same breath, and that is precisely the argument: **there is no room above this**. No older men in a warm chamber in the autumn weighing what happened here against three other things and calling it a draw; nothing today will be revised by anybody, because the man it would have to be revised with is the man they are chasing and he is the top of it. So it has to be all of it and it has to be now, and every part of that lands on the horses. Root asks his for the third long effort in an hour and gets it, which is the trouble with a good one — a bad horse tells you no at the second and saves itself, and a good one keeps producing what is asked until there is nothing behind the asking — and he feels the change in the breathing first and then in the way it comes back to his leg a beat late, and keeps going, because the alternative is a version of this that has to be done again in the spring. `prowess >= 12` and reckless/calm. Neither desc may name a realm, a crown, a title, a seat, a house or a place, or use the words *independent*, *sovereign*, *vassal* or *liege*; neither may produce a letter, a messenger or a document, the first being allowed its figure of speech in the title and nothing on the page permitted to cash it; and neither may use *ransom*, *quarter*, *terms*, *treaty* or *surrender*, or have anybody consider giving in, being spared or sparing, both pages being about the absence of a third party rather than about mercy. The first may not negotiate, rally, turn the line, blame anybody living or dead, say anything aloud, be wounded, end the day, or be congratulated on noticing. The second may not reach the far commander, see him, kill or take anybody, think about what root will be given for this, let the horse die, or end the day | 2 templates, base 50, 80 |
| what he paid to learn about going backwards | **The mod had never read a perk.** `has_perk` was at zero occurrences over a hundred and fourteen batches. An education trait is what was done to a boy, a focus is where a man's attention is this decade, and a perk is the third thing and the only one of the three that is a **purchase**: a node he spent years of accumulated experience on, deliberately, in preference to the node beside it. The two chosen are the opposite answers to one question, which is what makes them a pair rather than two unrelated nodes — `organized_retreat_perk` (`common/lifestyle_perks/00_martial_1_strategy_tree_perks.txt:96`) is a man who decided that knowing how to go backwards was worth paying for, and `never_back_down_perk` (`00_martial_3_chivalry_tree_perks.txt:229`) is a man who paid for the reverse conviction; both are confirmed present in AGOT's own copies, which is where those line numbers come from. The second carries the negation because the trees are not exclusive and a man with enough years in him can hold both, and when he does the first wins: having bought the knowledge of how to withdraw describes what he will actually do this afternoon, and the conviction is the thing that gets overruled by it. **Neither is a subset of the lifestyle or focus gates already shipped**, because a perk is permanent and a focus is not — `bc_he_was_pointed_elsewhere_and_came_back_trigger` reads `has_lifestyle = martial_lifestyle`, a statement about where attention is *now*, and a lord who bought a martial node at thirty and spends his fifties on books answers yes here and no there; the batch 113 focus gates are the same case in the other direction. Neither is `bc_he_was_pointed_at_war_and_left_trigger` either, which is about a boy pointed at arms who did not stay pointed at them, where these are about a specific sum of experience spent on a specific opinion. The commander half is `ordered`/`down`, only the second use of that pair in the mod and the only correct one here, and it is handed no modifier at all because `bc_pursuit` is for going forward. There is a moment he could put his finger on afterwards if anybody asked, which nobody will: the second push on the right goes in and does not come out, and the day stops being something he might win and becomes **a problem with a known shape**. He recognises the shape — he has recognised it perhaps two hundred times in his head, on roads, in the last hour before sleep, standing on hills at other men's battles with nothing whatever to do — and every one of those times cost him something to think all the way through, and this is the afternoon all of it was for. It is not a comfort. It is a set of instructions arriving in the right order without his having to assemble them: which rank goes and how far, at what interval, who stands while it goes, how long that standing is worth and the point at which it stops being worth it. The army comes off the ground in pieces that are still pieces, none of it looks like anything but a defeat because that is what it is, and **the whole of what separates it from the other kind of defeat is invisible from any distance and consists of about eleven decisions taken in the right sequence**. `martial >= 12` and calm/reckless. The knight half is `entrenched`/`down` and `bc_shieldwall` is the argument: he braces because bracing is the only thing the purchase told him to do. He has one answer and it has been a good answer for eleven years — whatever comes, be where you already are when it gets there and still be there afterwards — and he has made a small private religion of it, and it has paid him more often than not, and **it has no second part**. The answer works this afternoon precisely as well as it has ever worked and connects to nothing. He is not moved, the four men nearest him are not moved because he is not, and for a while there is an odd immovable lump in a line otherwise going quietly sideways; then the ends come round. He stays, and it is not courage by that point in the day, it is the only instruction he owns still running in the middle of a situation it was never about. `prowess >= 12` and stubborn/craven. Neither desc may stage the learning — no teacher, no master-at-arms, no yard, no book, no named campaign, nobody remembered — or use the words *lesson*, *training*, *drill*, *discipline* or *veteran*, or price anything in coin, the knight's title being allowed its figure of speech and neither page permitted to cash it. Neither may rally, turn the line, save the army, end the day, or have anybody praise or thank either man. The first may not name an enemy, speak to a named man, be wounded, doubt himself, feel satisfaction larger than the small flat kind, or produce a plan a reader could copy out. The second may not kill anybody, die, decide anything, be told he is right, think about the man beside him as a friend, or let the holding matter to the outcome | 2 templates, base 50, 80 |
| how his people fight, as opposed to who they are | The mod has asked **who** a man's people are three times and had never asked **how they fight**. `bc_he_is_free_folk_trigger` and the westerosi wrapper name a people, `bc_foe_is_countryman_trigger` asks whether two men share one, and all three are questions of identity; `has_cultural_tradition` was at zero occurrences and asks a question of method, and the two are orthogonal, since a Westerosi culture can hold either of these and so can a culture from anywhere else. **The trigger is culture-scoped, not character-scoped** — the 1.19.0.6 dump gives its form as `<culture> = { has_cultural_tradition = scope:tradition }` — so both gates spell `culture = { ... }` around it, exactly as `bc_he_is_free_folk_trigger` already does for `agot_is_wildling_culture`; a bare `has_cultural_tradition` on a character would not be a narrower gate, it would be a broken one. `culture` takes no guard: every character has one, and the file already reads it unguarded in three places. The two chosen are vanilla's own matched pair, `tradition_stand_and_fight` and `tradition_hit_and_run` (`common/culture/traditions/00_combat_traditions.txt:2516` and `:2391` in AGOT's copy), which are the two opposite answers to the question of what a people does when it meets an army. **They are not engine-exclusive, which is why the second carries a negation**: AGOT's copy sets `is_shown = { always = yes }` on both and has commented out the exclusivity clauses vanilla shipped, so a culture holding both would answer yes twice — and when it does the standing tradition wins, because every moment in this mod happens inside a set-piece battle, which is the situation `tradition_stand_and_fight` is about, and the raiding tradition describes what those same people do on all the days they are not in one. The commander half is `entrenched`/`up` and takes `bc_shieldwall` for winning by doing the plainest available thing very well. Nothing he has done since noon would be worth writing down: the line is where it was an hour ago, the second rank has gone forward twice by a few yards and come back to the same few yards, and **the entire substance of his afternoon has been to decline three separate opportunities to be clever**, which is a great deal harder than it sounds and he is probably the only man on the field who knows how hard. There is a thing that happens when a plain arrangement starts to work — the working of it feels like an invitation, and the invitation is always to move something somewhere — and he has watched better men than himself accept it and seen what accepting it costs. What he has instead is flat, unglamorous and older than any account of it: that the ground under your feet is the ground the argument is about, and that the man who moves first has answered a question nobody put to him. `martial >= 12` and brave/craven. The knight half is `wild`/`down` and `bc_spent_horse` is the argument, the animal being what gets used up when a man keeps trying to make room in a place that has none. Everything he has ever been good at starts with leaving — come in at the loose edge of a thing, take what is loose, be half a mile off before the answer arrives — and that is not a trick he was shown but the shape war has in his head. **It needs one condition and only one, which is somewhere to go, and there is nowhere to go.** This line has both ends fastened to other lines and is folding inward, so he takes the horse out along the inside of it hunting for an edge, finds none, comes back, goes out the other way, finds none there either; nobody has ordered any of it and nobody has forbidden it. By the third time out the horse is not giving him what it gave him the first time, and he can feel with complete precision how much of it he has used and how little he has bought, and he goes a fourth time regardless, because the alternative is to stand still in a rank and be a thing that gets hit, and he has no idea how to be that. `prowess >= 12` and impatient/patient. Neither desc may name a people, a tongue, a homeland, a region, a river or a road, or use the words *custom*, *tradition*, *ancestors*, *forefathers* or *our way*; and neither may have anybody sing, shout a war cry, invoke anything, or say a sentence about what their people do. The first may not compare his people to the far side, be proud of them, be admired, rally, turn the line, speak to a named man, end the day, or produce a plan a reader could copy out. The second may not find the gap, get out, kill anybody, let the horse die or fall, blame his commander, be ordered to stop, or end the day | 2 templates, base 50, 80 |
| the names that already mean an argument | The mod reads houses as a relation and as a count and has never read **which house**. `bc_foe_is_house_trigger` asks whether two men share a name and batch 111 asks how many people are left holding one; neither has ever asked what a particular name already meant before anybody on this field was born. The axis is `agot_is_historical_feud_trigger` (`00_agot_dynasty_triggers.txt:33`), **AGOT's own list** of the five house pairs whose quarrels predate the bookmark — Peake and Manderly, Jast and Falwell, Whitehill and Forrester, Yronwood and Fowler, Blackwood and Bracken — used rather than a hand-written `OR` of dynasty keys for the usual reason: if AGOT ever adds a sixth pair, both gates follow it without being touched. **The guard is not optional and it is not AGOT's.** Read the trigger: it is a bare `dynasty = dynasty:dynn_Peake` and the rest with no `?=` anywhere, in a file where the trigger directly beneath it spells `dynasty ?=` for the same kind of test, and this setting is full of hedge knights and sellswords with no dynasty at all — so both gates put the standing `exists = dynasty` in front of the call and nest the call in a `trigger_if` that repeats it. **Neither gate claims the other half of the feud is on the field.** The AGOT trigger answers a question about one man's name and nothing about who is opposite him, and a Blackwood facing a Forrester answers yes twice without being a feud at all, so both descs are written to what the gate actually says and neither may assert the counterpart is here. Disjoint by construction, the first winning the tie, because being the man with the name is a larger fact about his afternoon than noticing the name on somebody else. The commander half is `measured`/`up` and falls through the verb table, which is correct: there is no army modifier for having a surname. There is a thing his name does in a room and it was doing it two centuries before anybody thought of him — he did not build it, he cannot amend it, and it is an inheritance in the way a debt is an inheritance, arriving complete and deciding in advance how a certain sort of conversation will go before either party has opened it. **None of that has anything to do with what he is doing today.** This is a field and a hedge and an hour of the afternoon, and the argument being settled here is going his way because the ground was chosen well and the second line went in when it was asked to; the name is in his ear the whole time and contributes precisely nothing, and it is a small private pleasure to notice, between one order and the next, that today is being decided by things he actually did. `diplomacy >= 12` and cynical/content. The knight half is `ordered`/`down`, the pair the table deliberately leaves empty, so the rung and the stress are the whole consequence. The man they follow carries one of those names, everybody knows it, and **none of it is aimed anywhere near this knight** — he can feel that in the way they come on, briskly and competently, without the extra half-inch that arrives when a blow has something personal loaded into it. They put him back a pace and take it, and another and take that, all quite reasonable and quite thorough and it does not stop; his line gives ground the way a door gives ground, straight and grudging and without gaps, and that is the only thing he has to be pleased about. What sits oddly is that whatever those men are truly angry about is not here at all and will still be waiting for somebody else long after this hedge is forgotten. He is simply what is in front of them. `prowess >= 12` and vengeful/calm. Neither desc may name a house, a sigil, a seat, a river, a road or a person, or narrate where the quarrel came from, or put the counterpart on this field. The first may not be recognised, say its own name, rally, turn the line, speak to a named man, or end the day. The second may not reach the far commander, see his face, kill anybody, get out, have the situation explained to him, end the day, or take the far side's indifference as an insult he intends to answer later | 2 templates, base 50, 80 |
| the men on his payroll who are not here | **The mod has never read a court.** It reads what a man holds, who he is related to, what he was taught, what he believes, what he has vowed and what he is owed; `employs_court_position` was at zero occurrences and asks something none of those ask, which is what work he has decided is worth paying somebody else to do all year. Both positions are AGOT's own and both keys were read out of AGOT's file rather than guessed — `master_at_arms_court_position` (`00_agot_court_positions.txt:2965`) and `maester_court_position` (`:31`). The engine trigger takes the position key directly, exactly as AGOT spells it at `activities/activity_types/agot_sailing.txt:177`, and it is a character-scoped test rather than a link read, so **no guard**. **There is no negation between the two, and that is a decision**: these are not opposite halves of one question but two different facts, a lord may perfectly well pay for both, and what keeps them apart is the engine — the first is a commander template, the second is a knight template, the side commander is not in the knight list, and the two pools roll separately. Rarity is double on each: a landed ruler who has actually filled the seat, plus the cultural innovation AGOT hides each position behind (`innovation_agot_sword_bound` and `innovation_maesters`). The commander half is `entrenched`/`down` and **takes `bc_shieldwall` while losing**, which is the point: the rank has not burst, and the reason it has not burst is a year of somebody else's patient work in a yard he is nowhere near. Nothing anybody is deciding — the shoulder going into the shield at the same moment as the foot in men too tired to be choosing it, the second rank taking a half-step across untold when a gap opens, the two men closing on a man who goes down without either of them looking. **He can spend it and he cannot add to it**, and the arithmetic of that sits behind his teeth while he watches the line back away from the pressure without breaking: it is going to run out before the daylight does. `martial >= 12` and diligent/lazy. The knight half is `measured`/`up` and falls through, because nothing is being done to his army — he is winning and noticing something about the shape of the record. **This is not the station axis**: `bc_the_crown_on_the_field` and `bc_nothing_but_the_horse` read title tier and `is_landed` on a commander, and no knight template has ever read tenure at all; this one reads a household expense, which implies tenure the way a stable implies a horse. He is a man with tenants and a roof and an account of what the roof cost, and he is also forty minutes into pushing a shield into somebody's chest with a good deal of success, and in the gap between one press and the next the two lives touch: **all of it will end up somewhere behind him as a short entry with a date on it, and the entry will be correct, and it will contain nothing.** Not a lie — simply nothing that is happening. He does not resent it and is not sure he would want it otherwise. `stewardship >= 12` and patient/impatient. Neither desc may name the paid man, describe his face, give him a line of speech, put him on the field, or treat him as a person with an opinion about today; neither may quote a letter, read one aloud, or have anybody arrive with one. The first may not rally, turn the day, decide anything about the man it pays, be thanked or blamed, speak to a named man, or end the day. The second may not kill a named man, reach any commander, be praised, imagine the words of the record, end the day, or be pleased with itself | 2 templates, base 50, 80 |
| the arrangement that has not happened yet | `is_betrothed` returned zero over the whole mod. The mod reads marriages at length — a living wife, a pregnant one, a buried one, a wife's father in the far line, a marriage into the other side — and has never once read **the state that comes before all of that**, which is a contract signed by other people about a wedding that has not happened. Neither gate is a subset of the marriage gates: a betrothal is not a marriage, and a betrothed widower answering yes to a widower gate and yes to this one is two true facts about one man rather than a containment either way. The second walks `scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander` and so takes the standing guard, the `exists` sibling plus a `trigger_if` that repeats it; `is_betrothed` itself is a plain character test and needs nothing. **The second is also knowable, which is what licenses what is written on it**: a betrothal in this setting is negotiated between houses, announced, and talked about for a year, so a man in the line may know who the lord opposite is promised to without ever having been near him. Disjoint by construction, the first winning the tie, because the promise a man carries himself is a larger fact about his afternoon than the promise somebody else carries. Rarity: most adults with anything to command are married already and most betrothals belong to children who are in no battle. The commander half is `bloodied`/`down` and takes `bc_bloodied`, because this is the hour that costs and **the outstanding contract is what makes the cost legible** — without it the afternoon would only be expensive. He has started pricing it, which is a habit he cannot switch off and which is not helping, and the price keeps landing on the same item: an agreement made by men in a warm room over a season of letters, every clause drafted on the flat assumption that he would be a living man in the spring standing where he was expected to stand. **It is not a hope, it is a schedule**, and other households have already begun arranging themselves around it, none of whom know this hedge exists. He is bleeding it away by the quarter-hour and the odd part is how orderly it feels — not a disaster, a slow correction, one part of a document going grey at a time. `stewardship >= 12` and ambitious/content. The knight half is `wild`/`up`, so he takes the rung and `bc_spent_horse` in the same breath, and the reason he keeps going forward is a thought rather than an order. Somewhere a hall is being aired and somebody is doing sums about barrels, and **there are people alive today whose whole autumn is being organised around an event the last two hours have been a sustained and quite well-managed attempt to prevent.** He finds it funny in a hard, useless way, and it is what makes him take the horse forward one more time than the situation asks for, and then again; the animal is telling him plainly what it costs, and he is not going to get anywhere near the far end of this field and knows it, and goes anyway, because the alternative is to stop moving and be a man who noticed a joke. `prowess >= 12` and callous/compassionate. Neither desc may name the other party to the match, describe her, give her a house, seat, age or face, have anybody speak the arrangement aloud, quote a contract, or arrive with news of one; and **neither may settle the match in either direction**, because the gate says a promise is outstanding and says nothing about how it ends. The first may not rally, turn the day, speak to a named man, be consoled, or end the day. The second may not reach the far commander, see him, kill him, kill a named man at all, be ordered to stop, be admired, let the horse die or fall, or end the day | 2 templates, base 50, 80 |
| the slow thing that is running while he is here | `any_scheme` and `any_targeting_scheme` both returned zero over the whole mod. The mod reads a man's hatreds, friendships, secrets and hooks, and has never read **the one thing that is actually in motion** — a plot with a clock on it, running in some other part of the world, while he spends an afternoon in a hedge. Both are iterators and iterators are not links, so neither takes a guard: an empty list is a clean no. `scheme_type = murder` is read in scheme scope and is vanilla's own idiom (`00_scheme_interactions.txt:1582` and `:2038` spell exactly this inside `any_targeting_scheme`), and the key `murder` is the first line of `common/schemes/scheme_types/murder_scheme.txt` in vanilla **and in AGOT's override of the same file**, so the type survives AGOT. **Murder and not any scheme at all**: `any_scheme = { count >= 1 }` would fire for a large fraction of the AI, which is not a gate, and restricting it to the one type that intends a death makes both halves narrow and makes both mean something specific on a battlefield. No negation between them, and that is a decision — a man may be both plotter and target, and what keeps the templates apart is the engine, exactly as in batches 113 and 118. **The second is a fact its subject does not have**: a scheme targeting a man is not something he is told about, so the toast is forbidden from letting him find out, suspect, or name anybody; the mod has narrated around a man before and this is the same move with more at stake. The commander half is `ordered`/`up` and takes `bc_pursuit`, which is the point — he presses because a win here makes the other, quieter thing cheaper, and pressing is a decision rather than a mood. The order is the right one and also the cheap one; there is a version of the afternoon he could have run more carefully and he is not running it, because **there is a second thing of his in the world, patient and unhurried and older than this campaign, which does not require an army and will not be written down anywhere.** A loud win makes it easier by a little: men become less careful when they are frightened of an army, households look outward, and the attention of everybody who matters gets pulled onto the wrong subject for a season. He sits his horse with an expression four hundred men are reading as satisfaction, and none of them are reading it correctly. `intrigue >= 12` and vengeful/trusting. The knight half is `wild`/`down` and takes `bc_spent_horse`: he is on the wrong end of the day and using the animal up staying in it. **He is having the worst hour of his life and it is not the dangerous one**, and he has no access to that whatever — somewhere else entirely, no army near it and nothing that would look like anything to a man riding past, a thing has been arranged about him and is going forward at its own pace, and it will still be there next month whether he wins here, loses here or is carried off it. He fights the wrong danger with everything he has, which is not a mistake, because nobody has ever told him there is another one. `prowess >= 12` and reckless/paranoid. Neither desc may name anybody involved, place the plot on a map, name a house or hall, give a method, name a poison or a weapon, advance the business by a step, or have anybody on the field say one word about it. The first may not send, write or receive anything, check on it, worry about it, feel guilty about it, decide anything about it, rally, speak to a named man, or end the day. The second may not let him find out or suspect, may not kill or wound him, may not let the horse die or fall, may not have him get out, and may not end the day | 2 templates, base 50, 80 |
| the other thing that is killing people this year | Every epidemic keyword returned zero over the whole mod. It reads wars, feuds, factions, plots, oaths and weather, and has **never once read the other mechanism in this game that empties a hall**. `any_character_epidemic` is character-scoped and yields epidemic scope, and AGOT uses the system — `00_agot_epidemics_triggers.txt` is nine of its own triggers built on top of it — so this is not a vanilla feature the submod is pretending exists in Westeros. Iterators are not links, so the first gate takes no guard and an empty list is a clean no; vanilla spells the same test as `any_character_epidemic = { }` at `events/dlc/ep3/ep3_emperor_yearly_8.txt:119` and this file's house style is the explicit `count >= 1`. The second walks `scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander` and takes the standing guard. **The second is visible, which is what licenses what is written on it**: nobody in a line knows the name of a sickness or where it started, but what a man can see from fifty paces is a host that moves wrong. Disjoint by construction, the first winning the tie, because during a real outbreak both hosts will often answer yes and the thing in his own camp is the larger fact. Rarity: epidemics are episodic rather than ambient, most years in most realms have none, and then it has to be running on the day. The commander half is `bloodied`/`down` and takes `bc_bloodied`, which is exactly right — the modifier is the one for a host that has been reduced, and **the point of the moment is that a good part of the reduction happened before the first order of the day was given.** The file that ought to be twelve deep is eight; two companies took an hour longer than they should have to come up and are moving like men at the end of a long day; the ones who are here are standing in the correct place with nothing behind the standing. It is not cowardice and it is not the far side. He tries the manoeuvre once because the situation demands it be tried, it comes apart in the middle in a way it would not have in the spring, and he stops asking — **there is no order that gets him the missing men back.** `martial >= 12` and paranoid/trusting. The knight half is `adapted`/`up` and falls through, because there is no army modifier that means *he worked out why they are slow*, and there should not be. It takes him three passes to see it and when he has it he wishes slightly that he had not: they are not breaking, breaking has a shape and this is not it — **the far rank keeps arriving at each moment a beat and a half after the moment needs them**, the shield up late, the step across never coming, a man in the second file sitting down with nobody near enough to have put him there. He has seen a body of men move like that once before, in a place he does not much like remembering. So it will be a victory, and he already knows exactly what it is worth, which is a good deal less than the men shouting on his left believe, and he keeps his mouth shut about why. `learning >= 12` and arrogant/compassionate. Neither desc may name a sickness, describe a symptom clinically, give it a cause, origin, direction of travel or cure, blame anybody, produce a physician or healer of any kind, have anybody die of it on the page, or use the words *plague*, *fever*, *pestilence* or *contagion*. The first may not rally, turn the day, send anybody home, make a decision about the sick, be reported to, or end the day. The second may not reach the far commander, catch anything, be told anything, kill a named man, pity them aloud, gloat, slow down out of decency, or end the day | 2 templates, base 50, 80 |
| the other host that has his name on it | `any_army` returned zero over the whole mod. A hundred and twenty-one batches have read the men in front of a commander, the men beside him, the ground under him and the war he is inside, and not one of them has ever asked the simplest strategic question there is: **whether this field is the whole of what he currently has in the world, or one of two places his name is being spent today.** The 1.19.0.6 dump gives `any_army` as "Iterate through all armies", character scope, army target. The test is `count >= 2` and not `count >= 1`, because the host standing on this field is itself one of the entries and one would be true of every raiser in every battle — two is the smallest number that means what the moment is about. Iterators are not links, so the first gate takes no guard; the second walks `scope:combat_side.enemy_side.side_commander` and takes the standing one. **This is not an odds gate.** `bc_their_host_gap_value` and the side-size rows read how many men are on this field; this reads whether there is a second field at all, which is a fact about a campaign rather than a fact about a battle, and the two are independent in both directions — a man may be outnumbered here and have nothing anywhere else, or hold the larger host here and still have half his strength sitting in front of somebody's gate a week's ride away. The second gate is rumour and is written as rumour: where the far lord's other host is and what it is doing cannot be known from a rank, but *that there is one* is exactly the sort of thing a camp knows, because armies are raised in public and men on the roads talk. Disjoint by construction, the first winning the tie, since both sides in a large war will often answer yes and having half of one's own strength elsewhere is the larger fact about a man's afternoon. Rarity: most raisers hold one host at a time, a second raised army standing somewhere else on the same day is a middle-of-a-big-war thing, and then the man has to be in a battle. The commander half is `adapted`/`down` and falls through the verb table, which is correct twice over — nothing is being done to the army that a modifier could describe, and what is actually happening is a revision. He is watching his centre give for the third time and the thought arrives without any drama at all, in the same flat voice he uses on himself for questions about grain: **this is not the only thing he has going.** The sum reorganises itself while men are shouting — what is standing on this grass is worth less to the year than what is standing elsewhere, and the elsewhere needs nothing from him this afternoon except that he not do something stupid here in the next hour. It changes none of the orders. It takes the panic out of the arithmetic and leaves something colder in its place, which is that **he can survive today being a defeat and could not have survived it being a rout**, and the difference is entirely his to manage. `stewardship >= 12`, because holding two hosts at once is an accounting problem before it is a martial one, and calm/reckless. The knight half is `bloodied`/`up` on a winning side — the rank is going forward and being taken apart while it does. He does the work, takes his share of what going forward costs, and takes the wound in his forearm that he will remember longer than the victory. What he cannot make himself believe, while everybody within ten yards behaves as though the war ends when this rank does, is the thing they have all decided: that the lord opposite is finished when this host is. **What they are paying for in the middle of this field is one host and not one enemy.** Nobody knows where the other one is and it does not matter. So he goes forward, and thinks, with no bitterness and no intention of saying it to anybody, that they are going to do this again. `martial >= 12`, because knowing that a broken host is not a beaten enemy is the one piece of strategy a man in a rank is in a position to have learned, and patient/arrogant. Neither desc may say where the other host is, who leads it, what it is besieging or marching to, how many men are in it, which way it is facing, or when it moves; neither may have a rider arrive or a message go out, name a place, road, crossing, castle or town, or count anything in thousands. The first may not withdraw, order a withdrawal, rally, turn the day, abandon anybody by name, be relieved, or end the day. Every one will be a price, and a season of another family arranging itself quietly around a gap, and he knows precisely how that goes from both ends because he has stood at both. **It does not stop him and it does not slow him.** `diplomacy >= 12`, because a prisoner is a negotiation that runs for a season and the men who are good at those are the ones who see them coming, and cynical/compassionate. The knight half is `bloodied`/`down` and takes `bc_bloodied`. There is already one of his behind a door — long enough that the family has stopped saying so aloud and started arranging itself around the gap, the way a household arranges itself around a chair nobody sits in any more — and he knows what it costs to the last stag, not because anybody told him but because he watched what the finding of it did to the man who had to find it. **Nobody over there is trying to kill him**, and that is the part men outside a line never understand: someone in good mail with a name on his surcoat is not a corpse, he is a sum, and every man in front of him would infinitely rather have the sum. **Which is the entire reason he will not go down** — not for courage, not for the banner, not for anything that would sound well repeated afterwards by people who were elsewhere, but because there is exactly one thing worse for his people than losing today, and it is losing today and then being told there is a second one to be paid for. `prowess >= 12`, because what is measured is the capacity to keep paying for an hour that is already lost, and stubborn/craven. Neither desc may name a place, have a rider arrive or a message go out, or count in thousands. The first may not name a prisoner, say who or where any of them is or what any is worth, and nobody may be ransomed, released, killed, moved or spoken to. The second may not name the relation or the man holding him, say where the cell is or how long it has been, price the ransom, rescue anybody or promise to, reach the far commander, kill a named man, be told anything, or end the day | 2 templates, base 50, 80 |
| what the marriage is actually like | The marriage axis opened at batch 88 and has been worked several times since — the pregnancy row, the two burial rows, the married-into-the-other-side row — and every one of them reads the marriage as an *arrangement*: who it joined, what it produced, whether it ended. None of them ever read **what it is like**. AGOT keeps two scripted relations for precisely that and the mod had touched neither: `unfruitful_marriage` zero, `kicked_from_marriage_bed` zero, `primary_spouse` zero. **Both are AGOT's own and both are `hidden = yes`** — they sit at `common/scripted_relations/00_agot_scripted_relations.txt:15` and `:52`, each reciprocal with itself, and the first carries `fertility = -5`, which is the mod saying in its own hand what it means by it. Being hidden, neither ever appears in the character interface: they are facts the game keeps and never shows, which is exactly the register this file wants. AGOT sets the first at `common/on_action/marriage_concubinage.txt:189` and reads both live at `common/scripted_triggers/00_agot_lmf_triggers.txt:425` and `:427`, and `events/agot_lmf_events/agot_lmf_spouse_events.txt:5132` reads the second against `primary_spouse`, which is the exact form used here. **Both gates take the guard**: `primary_spouse` is an optional link and reading it as a target is a read, so each carries `exists = primary_spouse` as a sibling and repeats it in a `trigger_if` limit. **Neither is a subset of any shipped marriage gate** — batch 88's header already records why a bare `is_married = yes` is not a gate, no such gate was ever shipped, and the rows that exist require a pregnancy, or a dead former spouse, or a marriage across the battle line, none of which either relation implies. **Two facts, two men, and they are not the same unhappiness**: one marriage is still a marriage and has produced nothing, the other has stopped being one in the only room where that is measured, and a man may have either without the other. The commander half is `wild`/`down` and takes `bc_spent_horse`. **Eleven years, and the room upstairs is precisely as quiet as it was in the first of them** — nobody mentions it, not his steward, not his septon, not her, least of all her, and the silence has been so complete for so long that he suspects it is the loudest thing in his household. It ought to have no bearing whatever on a field. And yet here he stands with his flank folding and his reserve spent, and **the ordinary consolation of his class is simply not available to him**: other men can afford an afternoon like this one because the losing of it is survivable, because there is somebody small asleep in a hall a long way off who will be told the story in twenty years by a man paid to make it sound better than it was. He has no such person. **So the next hour is not a wager on his line — it is the line**, beginning and end, standing in one tired body on one bad slope, and he asks the men nearest him for more than they have left and asks the same of himself. `intrigue >= 12`, because a man without an heir knows exactly which of his relations gains by a bad hour and that is not a martial thought, and paranoid/trusting. The knight half is `wild`/`up` and takes `bc_spent_horse`, which is literal here. It is going well, and the men around him have the loose quick look of people who can already taste the end of it, and **twice now he has caught himself easing his horse a little wide of the shortest line and twice told himself it was the ground**. It is not the ground. There is a door at home that he does not open — nobody announced it, there was no scene worth repeating, only a night that finished in a different room and then a great many more that finished the same way, and a household absorbing it the way households absorb everything, by never once referring to it. **Not one man out here knows, and that is the particular quality of this arrangement: it is invisible, it is meant to be**, and the same men who would pity him openly for a limp or a bad debt have no idea there is anything to pity. So he asks more of the horse than the moment needs and stays out in the one hour of his life where nothing has to be explained to anybody, and the animal will present the account tomorrow, and he knows it, and does it anyway. `prowess >= 12`, because staying out past the point the moment requires is paid for in the body, and ambitious/content. Neither desc may name a place, have a rider arrive or a message go out, or count in thousands. The first may not name her, quote her, put her in the scene, blame her or himself in so many words, name a physician, a septon or a remedy, or name any relation who stands to gain. **The second may not name her either**, may not stage the night it started, may not give it a cause, and may not let one person on this field know about it, reach the far commander, kill a named man, be told anything, or end the day | 2 templates, base 50, 80 |
| how much appetite each of them actually has | The whole AI-personality surface returned zero over the mod — `ai_boldness`, `ai_energy`, `ai_zeal`, `ai_greed`, `ai_compassion`, `ai_honor`, `ai_rationality`, `ai_vengefulness`, `ai_sociability`. A hundred and thirty batches have read traits, skills, stress, dread, opinion and tyranny, and not one of them ever read **the number the engine itself uses to decide whether a man will risk something**. **The dump is wrong about the second keyword and the live files are right**: 1.19.0.6 documents `dread_modified_ai_boldness = { character = root value >= 5 }`, and that parameter name does not appear in a single shipped file. Vanilla writes `dreaded_character` sixty-seven times — `common/scripted_effects/00_interaction_effects.txt:733` has `dreaded_character = scope:actor` with `value <= -50` under the comment *"Too scared to do anything"* — and AGOT writes it too, at `common/character_interactions/00_agot_spy_network_interactions.txt:421` with `value < -20` and `:426` with `value < -45`. The live form is what ships here; this is the second time the dump has been the less reliable of the two, and the note is worth keeping. **The scale is signed, not zero-to-a-hundred**: vanilla's own `common/script_values/00_ai_values.txt:25` sets `high_positive_ai_value = 50` and the dread-modified reads go to −20, −45 and −50, so the range runs roughly −100 to 100 with the mass in the middle, and both thresholds here are vanilla's constants rather than invented ones. **Both gates are root-independent on purpose** — batch 127's header records why — so every character is named through `scope:combat_side`, which the knight templates already rely on to reach their own commander. **Both take the guard, and the first takes two**, since naming an optional link as the target of `dreaded_character` is a read. **This is not the dread rows**: every shipped dread read is `dread` on the scoped man himself, how frightening he is to everybody in general, while this asks what that frightfulness has actually done to one specific person's willingness to gamble today — a different number, which can be small while his dread is enormous or large while it is nothing. The commander half is `entrenched`/`up` and takes `bc_shieldwall`, because standing still is the whole of the action. **You can see it in what the other man is not doing**: he has the ground for a push and is not pushing, he has had two openings since noon and took neither, and both times the thing that stopped him was not the slope and not his men but a piece of arithmetic he was doing about a name, and the name was this one. **It is a strange thing to be, another man's reason for hesitating** — nobody builds that on purpose, it gets built out of a decade of specific afternoons most of which he would rather not walk through again, and it does not make him proud so much as tired, and useful. So he does the only thing this requires and it is nearly nothing: sets his feet, has the line dressed, and declines every invitation to be the one who commits first. **Over there a man entirely capable of winning this is going to spend the next hour talking himself out of it**, and the whole of that hour is a gift handed across by somebody who does not know he is handing it, and who will remember this afternoon for the rest of his life as the one where the ground was wrong. `diplomacy >= 12`, because what is being read is a person and not a field, and patient/arrogant. The knight half is `ordered`/`down` and falls through the verb table on purpose — the order has not arrived, nothing has been done to either army, and the whole moment is the waiting. **There is an order coming and he can feel the shape of it the way a man feels weather.** This is the point in an afternoon where whoever is in charge either sets his teeth and buys a quarter of an hour with somebody's life or does the other thing, and he happens to know exactly which of those his own commander is. **Not a fool** — that is what people outside a line never grasp about men of that sort: not stupid, not vain, simply carrying a wider notion of what is worth attempting than most men do, and it has come off often enough to be the reason he stands where he stands. It has also failed, twice, and one of those times cost people the knight could name without stopping to think. **So he waits for a decision made by a man he trusts completely and would not wager a horse on**, and finds both of those true at the same moment, and that neither will alter one thing he does. He will go. Obviously he will go. `martial >= 12`, because predicting a commander is a professional's knowledge and not a friend's, and cynical/trusting. Neither desc may name a place, have a rider arrive or a message go out, or count in thousands. The first may not name the man opposite, quote him, reach him, hurt him, say what built the fear or when, or name a battle, a siege or a year. **The second may not let the order arrive** — nobody may be told anything, nobody may shout, no decision may be made by anybody, and it must still be the waiting when the desc stops — and may not name the commander, name the two occasions it went wrong, reach the far commander, kill a named man, or end the day | 2 templates, base 50, 80 |
