| who is leading the column and who is only in it | The whole travel surface returned zero over the mod — `is_travelling`, `any_travel_plan`, `is_travel_leader` and `is_travel_entourage_character`. That is a large silence: travel is one of the few states a character can be in for months at a stretch, and a hundred and thirty-two batches have read what a man owns, whom he is related to and what he believes without once asking whether he is at this moment a long way from where he sleeps. The pair splits the same road two ways. A travelling party has exactly one leader and any number of men riding in his train, and those are not the same experience of the same journey. **They are disjoint by construction, and they have to be**: the engine is explicit that the travel leader is himself part of the entourage, so the second gate would be a strict superset of the first if left alone, and it is not left alone — it carries `is_travel_leader = no` as a sibling, which cuts the leader back out. A man passes exactly one of these or neither, never both. Both read forms are in shipped files rather than only in the dump: vanilla writes `is_travel_leader = yes` at `common/customizable_localization/00_relations.txt:293` and AGOT copies it into `00_agot_lmf_relations_custom_loc.txt:296`, and vanilla writes `is_travel_entourage_character` at `common/on_action/travel_on_actions.txt:1172`. **Neither takes a guard**, both being plain booleans on the scoped character rather than links. **This is not the home and capital rows**: those ask where a man's holdings are and whether he is fighting near them, which is true of him whether he is standing in his own hall or two kingdoms away, while this asks whether he is in motion — whether there is a column somewhere with his tents on it that expects to be elsewhere by the end of the week. A man can be fighting on his own doorstep and still be mid-journey, and very far from home and perfectly stationary. The commander half is `entrenched`/`up` and takes `bc_shieldwall`. **Being the reason this many men were in one place, moving, turns out not to be a different job from this one** — it is the same job with the shouting brought forward, and everything he does now he has already done on foot at dawn in bad temper: how long a body keeps going before it stops being useful, which complaint is a real one, whether the thing in front of him is a problem or merely unpleasant. **The useful part is not that they trust him, it is that they have stopped checking** — when he tells the second rank to close up they close up without the small hesitation that costs a line its shape, because a man who has told them where to sleep for a month has earned the right to tell them where to stand for an hour. He notices the far end holding better than it has any business holding, understands why, and does not say so, because saying so would spoil it. **The only thing left to do is not become a different man from the one they have been following.** `martial >= 12`, and diligent/lazy. The knight half is `bloodied`/`down`. **He is fighting in what happened to be on him** — not what he chose, not what he paid for, the good stuff being strapped under canvas going somewhere at the pace of oxen, and nobody consulted him about that either. What he has instead of the gear is the arithmetic: this much protects him, that much does not, and here are the four places on his body where the answer is no. **Every adjustment he has made costs him speed at the moment when slow is the whole problem** — standing a half-pace closer than he likes, weight further back, taking things on the flat rather than turning them the way he would with the right gear on his arm. And the thought that keeps surfacing, uninvited and useless, is that not one of the men breaking on his right knows he is doing this at half his weight, and none of them would care if they did, **and he is not sure they should**. `prowess >= 12`, and stubborn/craven. Neither desc may name a place, a river crossing, a castle or a town, have a rider arrive or a message go out, or count in thousands. The first may not name the destination, the road, the country he set out from or the reason for the journey; nobody may arrive, be sent ahead or back, or bring word; he may not withdraw, rally, turn the day or end it, and he may not be described as having chosen this ground. The second may not name the man he rides with, name his own house, complain in words to anybody or be answered, may not kill a named man, reach the far commander, recover his gear, be resupplied, or end the day, and nothing on the baggage may be described in detail | 2 templates, base 50, 80 |
| what the crown on this side is actually worth | `legitimacy`, `legitimacy_level`, `has_legitimacy` and `has_legitimacy_flag` all returned zero across the mod. AGOT uses the system - two hundred and eighty-three of its files mention it, and it reads `legitimacy_level` against 0, 1 and 2 in `common/activities/activity_types/feast.txt:754` and `agot_sailing.txt:558` - and this mod had never asked the one question the system exists to answer: whether the men around a crowned man think the crown is his by right. **`has_legitimacy = yes` is not decoration on the first gate, it is the gate.** `legitimacy_level` returns 0 for a character who does not use legitimacy at all, which is nearly everybody in a battle line, so `<= 1` written alone would be true of every landless knight and hedge swordsman on the field — and a gate that fires for nearly everybody is not a gate. The boolean cuts the population down to men the game actually scores; the threshold then does the work it was written for. It is not redundant and must not be deleted as such. Both keywords are in shipped files: `has_legitimacy = yes` at `common/character_interactions/00_dynast_interactions.txt:103`, and `legitimacy_level <= 1` at `common/activities/intents/chariot_race_intents.txt:331`. **The second gate is root-independent on purpose** for the reason batch 127 recorded, naming its man through `scope:combat_side` rather than assuming `root`, and takes the guard on `side_commander`. **The two gates read two different men and the negation is deliberately omitted** — the first is the man wearing the thin crown, the second a man in the line who has noticed that the person he follows wears one, and the pools roll separately. **This is not the dread rows or the opinion rows**: dread is how frightening a man is, opinion is how one specific person feels about him, and legitimacy is neither — it is whether the office is believed in, which can be catastrophic on a man who is both feared and liked and immaculate on a man nobody can stand. The commander half is `measured`/`down` and falls through the verb table, because nothing has been done to either army; the whole moment is a realisation. **These men came because of him — not because of what he holds.** Not one of them swore to an office, they came for things he did in front of them or for them or to somebody they hated, and every one of those debts is finite and all of them are being spent right now at a rate he cannot control. **A man followed for his office has something underneath him when the day goes wrong**: the office does not get tired, does not have a bad hour, and is still there in the evening whatever happened at noon. He has only the sum of what people privately think of him, and he can watch that sum getting smaller in the specific way the second rank looks at him and then does not. **And he cannot even be angry, because nobody has broken a word — they never gave him the other kind of word to break.** `diplomacy >= 12`, because what is being read is belief rather than ground, and paranoid/trusting. The knight half is `ordered`/`up` and takes `bc_pursuit`. **They are not convinced and they are not moving, and those are two different facts.** He has stood in lines that believed and lines that did not and can tell them apart from the inside without turning his head; this one does not believe, and he would be lying if he said no part of that was his. And it is holding. **Belief is not what keeps a rank together in the third hour** — what keeps it together is the man either side of you, and that stepping back is a decision you would have to make on purpose while two people watch, and thirty years of everyone agreeing that the ones who step back are the ones we do not speak to afterwards. All of that works without anybody being convinced of anything, and he can feel the enemy front coming apart under the same arithmetic applied to men with the same doubts and a worse hour. **A great deal of what people call loyalty is a habit that has not yet had a reason to stop.** `martial >= 12`, because telling a convinced line from a merely standing one is a professional's ear, and content/ambitious. Neither desc may name a place, have a rider arrive or a message go out, or count in thousands. The first may not name the office, the title, the seat, the man he took it from, the manner of the taking or any year, and no crown, circlet or chair may be described; nobody may renounce him, defect, refuse an order or say anything to him. The second may not name his commander, name his own house, name what is wrong with the claim, or discuss it in words with anybody; nobody may desert, be accused or be overheard; and he may not be spoken to by his commander | 2 templates, base 50, 80 |
| which direction the last peace ran | `any_truce_holder` and `any_truce_target` both returned zero over the mod, which is a real omission: a truce is the only object in this game that is a durable, dated record of a war somebody **lost**. It exists precisely because two men stopped, and it remembers which of them had to. A hundred and thirty-four batches have read wars, grudges, dread, opinion and kin without once reading the small legal residue of the last time this went badly for somebody. **The pair is the same object read from its two ends, and that is the point.** `any_truce_target` iterates the men this character holds one over — the people who are not allowed to come for him, because he beat them recently enough that the game is still counting. `any_truce_holder` iterates the men who hold one over him, which is the same fact told the other way round: he is the one who stopped. **Neither is a subset of the other and neither carries a negation**, since they point in opposite directions and a man who has fought both sides of a career can be in both lists without either statement becoming less true; and they read two different men from pools that roll separately, which is the reasoning batches 110, 113, 118, 120 and everything from 123 onward record. Both forms are in shipped files: `any_truce_holder` at `common/character_interactions/00_character_interactions.txt:437` in vanilla and `:496` in AGOT, `any_truce_target` at `common/decisions/dlc_decisions/mpo/mpo_decisions.txt:3244`. **Neither takes a guard**, both being iterators. **This is not the war rows**: those ask about the war he is standing in — how long it has run, who declared it, what it is for — and this asks about a different war that is already over, and about the paperwork it left. A man can be in the first year of a fresh war carrying three truces from the last one, and a man can fight for a decade without generating one, because truces come from settlements and not from fighting. The commander half is `adapted`/`up`. **Winning does not remove enemies, it only schedules them**, and he knows the schedule — could recite it standing here with his ears full of this, the way another man knows his children's ages: this one quiet until the harvest after next, that one longer, that one nearly out and lately very careful to be seen doing nothing. **Not one of them has forgiven anything. They are simply barred**, by an arrangement everybody signed and nobody meant, and that arrangement is the only reason his flank is not a problem right now. **What he is standing on this afternoon is not the ground and not the men but a set of dates**, which makes the whole thing feel less like a victory than like a lease — he is winning, the winning is real, and he is also aware that he is spending men and hours on one afternoon while three others sit in the future with his name on them, waiting for a season to turn. `intrigue >= 12`, because the calendar of who is barred and until when is not a soldier's knowledge, and callous/compassionate. The knight half is `wild`/`down` and takes `bc_spent_horse`. **Men who have never lost one think the bad part is the fear; it is not** — the bad part is that it happens slowly and you are awake for all of it. He recognises the stage they are at, recognised it twenty minutes ago, and has said nothing, because the men beside him have not got there yet and there is no benefit in their arriving early. **There is a version of him that could put his sword down at the correct moment and be perfectly alive in the morning, and he has met that version, and does not care for the acquaintance.** So he goes forward instead, harder than the situation warrants, into a part of the line that does not need him — and it is not courage, he would be embarrassed to have it called that; it is closer to being unwilling to sit through the whole thing a second time and know at every point exactly where he is. **The horse will not thank him for this**, and neither will anybody watching, if anybody is. `prowess >= 12`, and brave/craven. Neither desc may name a place, have a rider arrive or a message go out, or count in thousands. The first may not name any of the barred men, name what he took from them, name the settlement, state a term, or say when any of it runs out in months or years, and nobody may arrive, send word or break anything. The second may not name the man who holds it, name his own house, name the field where it happened, or describe the surrender itself; nobody may recognise him, taunt him or refer to it; and he may not surrender again or be captured | 2 templates, base 50, 80 |
| which of the two sides did not come alone | `any_war_ally` returned zero over the mod. A hundred and thirty-five batches have read this war's length, its cause, its declarer and its stakes without once asking the simplest question a man can ask standing in a line and looking left: is everybody here for the same reason, or has somebody else's banner turned up with its own arithmetic attached. **This is the first pair in the mod where the two gates differ by which army is being asked about rather than by which character.** The first asks whether the side the scoped man stands in has allies in it; the second asks whether the side opposite does. Both read through `scope:combat_side`, which makes them role-agnostic — nothing here is a property only a commander has, so either file may call either gate, and a knight reaches them exactly as he already reaches his own side's primary participant in the hook rows. **They are not mirrors and neither implies the other**: wars are frequently lopsided, one side a coalition and the other one furious man, and a side's ally count says nothing whatever about the other side's, so no negation is needed and none is written. **Both take the guard and the second walks two links**, so its `exists` rows come in a pair exactly as batch 131's dread-modified gate does; the iterator inside needs no guard of its own, but the scope it runs inside is a link and that link does. `any_war_ally` is in shipped files at `common/character_interactions/06_ep3_laamp_interactions.txt:4869` in vanilla and `00_agot_first_ranger_interactions.txt:54` in AGOT. **This is not the host-size rows or the outnumbered rows**: those count men, and allies are not men — a side with four allies can be smaller than a side with none, and the fact that matters is not the size of the line but that some part of it belongs to somebody who can go home whenever he decides the price has been paid. The commander half is `entrenched`/`down` and takes `bc_shieldwall`. **His own men will stand until he tells them not to, and some of them past that, and he has never had to think about why.** The rest came because a thing was owed and a thing was called in, and every man in it is standing inside an arrangement rather than a loyalty, and arrangements have an end that everybody can feel approaching. **They are not giving way — they are doing exactly what was agreed and doing it competently — it is that they are doing it like men fulfilling a term.** There is a difference between a rank that will hold and a rank that will hold until the debt is considered settled, and it is invisible from any distance and enormous from here. He is losing slowly, and slowly is survivable; what he does not know is **which of the men to his left has already privately decided what the debt was worth**, and what hour of the afternoon that decision comes due. **He cannot ask. Asking is the one thing that would make it happen.** `diplomacy >= 12`, and cynical/trusting. The knight half is `bloodied`/`up`. **He did not work it out by looking, because you cannot see anything from in here** — he worked it out because the pressure on his shield changed character four paces ago, and men who have trained together give way in the same direction and these did not: one part went back and the part beside it went sideways, and there is now a hand's width of daylight between them that neither of them owns. **Not a weakness exactly. A seam.** Two groups of perfectly good men who have never had to hold a line together, discovering right now that holding a line together is a skill and not an intention. His arm is opened along the outside and he has noticed and set it aside, because the arithmetic is favourable and he is not going to spoil it by being careful. **The seam cannot close while both halves are still expecting the other half to close it.** `martial >= 12`, because reading a join from inside the fighting is learned and not guessed, and patient/impatient. Neither desc may name a place, have a rider arrive or a message go out, or count in thousands. The first may not name an ally, name a house, name what was promised or paid for the help, or count the contingents, and nobody may leave the field, refuse an order or send to him. The second may not name any banner, house, sigil or colour, name his own side, or say who the enemy contingents belong to, and may not exploit the seam by name, be ordered into it, or tell anybody about it | 2 templates, base 50, 80 |
| what each of them is owed on paper | `any_claim`, `has_claim_on` and `is_claimant` all returned zero over the mod, which is a surprising gap in a setting built almost entirely out of claims. A hundred and thirty-six batches have read what a man holds, what he owes, whom he hates and where he sleeps without once reading the thing the wars are legally about: a piece of parchment saying something ought to have been his. **The two reads split on `pressed`, which is the only distinction that matters.** A pressed claim is one somebody is currently fighting over — it is in a war aim, men are dying for it today. An unpressed claim is the same parchment with nobody acting on it: true, filed, and doing nothing. The engine keeps both under one iterator and separates them with a parameter, and vanilla uses both values — `pressed = yes` at `common/scripted_triggers/00_courtier_guest_management_triggers.txt:643` and `pressed = no` at `common/script_values/destiny_score_interest_value.txt:473` — with AGOT shipping the same lines. **Neither is a subset of the other and neither needs a negation**: they are disjoint filters over one list, but a man can be in both results at once, holding one claim men are dying for today and three more nobody has mentioned in twenty years. **The thresholds are deliberately different numbers.** One pressed claim is already unusual, requiring an active war whose aim is that title. Unpressed claims accumulate — they arrive by inheritance, marriage and grant and never expire on their own — so one of them is nearly ambient among highborn men, and three is the number that makes the second gate mean a man carrying a folder rather than a man carrying a grievance. **Neither takes a guard**, both being iterators over the scoped character's own list. **This is not the war rows**: those ask about the war he is standing in from the outside, and this asks what is written in his own name whether or not anybody is doing anything about it — the second gate specifically about the titles nobody is doing anything about, which is the opposite of a war. The commander half is `adapted`/`down` and falls through the verb table, since nothing has been done to either army and the whole moment is a realisation. **He has read it more times than is dignified** — could recite the operative sentence, the two clauses that qualify it, and the phrase the other side pretends means something else, every word chosen by careful men in a warm room for exactly this contingency. **And none of it has any purchase out here.** The parchment does not care that his second line is bending; the field does not care what the parchment says. In an hour somebody will be able to state as a plain fact who was right, **and the mechanism by which that fact gets established is currently a lot of frightened men pushing at each other in the wrong order**. He had believed, in some unexamined way, that being correct was load-bearing — not a stupid belief, since everyone he has ever dealt with behaved as though it were true. **It is simply that this afternoon is the one procedure in which it does not come up at all**, and he is finding out about that while it happens to him. `learning >= 12`, and ambitious/content. The knight half is `wild`/`up` and takes `bc_spent_horse` alongside the rung, which is the whole point of calling it wild. **There are things that are his — not in the sense that he has them, in the older, colder sense**, written down somewhere, entirely true, and never in his lifetime the cause of a single hour's lost sleep for the men sitting on them. He stopped mentioning it long ago, because mentioning it makes people uncomfortable in a way that ends conversations, and because **the fourth time you say a thing out loud and nothing happens, you understand that saying it is not the mechanism**. Nothing is the mechanism, except this, and this almost never comes. And here it is: a line already going, men already leaning back, and no reason on the earth to be careful. **So he is not careful**, and it is not really about anybody in front of him, and he knows that, and it does not slow him at all. Nobody watching will connect the two — there is nothing to connect, it is a good knight fighting well on a good afternoon. **He will pay for the horse in the morning, and he would pay considerably more than that.** `prowess >= 12`, and reckless/calm. Neither desc may name a place, have a rider arrive or a message go out, or count in thousands. The first may not name the title, the seat, the man holding it, the document, the person who witnessed it or any year, and nobody may arrive, send word or concede anything. The ground triggers have asked what a field is **made of** since plan 1 — hills, bog, forest, the six bands — and never once asked what it **means**. In this setting that is a real omission: men fight on ground other men have walked to on purpose, and the difference between a hillside and a hillside somebody's grandmother was buried under is not a terrain type. **The pair is disjoint by construction and the negation is load-bearing.** Both gates require the field to be a holy site; the first then requires that it is *not* holy to the man leading this side, and the second that it is. The commander half is `ordered`/`down` and falls through the verb table, since nothing has been done to either army. **He looked at this place the way he looks at any place**: the fall of it, the footing, where a horse can turn and where it cannot — and it took him until well into the afternoon to understand he was the only man here reading it that way. The others came up this slope already knowing what it was. He can see it in how they fight, **which is not harder exactly — harder would be simpler — but with a kind of settledness**, as though giving way here would be a different and worse category of thing than giving way somewhere ordinary. **Nobody has said a word about it, and they do not need to say anything about it to each other, which is the entire problem**, and he has no equivalent to offer his own men because to him it is a field with a good slope. **He has spent his whole life being told terrain is the thing you cannot argue with; it turns out there is a second thing, older, sitting underneath the first**, and he walked his line onto it this morning without noticing. `learning >= 12`, and paranoid/arrogant. The knight half is `entrenched`/`up` and takes `bc_shieldwall`. **He has not said anything and does not intend to** — it would come out wrong and half the men around him would think he was making a claim about himself. What he has instead is the thing his feet are doing, which is not moving: **not one boot-length, not the little accommodating shuffle a line makes a hundred times an hour without anybody deciding to**. He has always known where he is, and that knowledge sat in the back of his head all morning while he checked straps and complained about the wait like everybody else, and it has quietly changed what he is willing to do. The push comes and he does not go back; it comes again and he still does not, **and the two men either side of him find themselves not going back either, because that is how a line works — it is nobody's decision, it is a series of small ones made by whoever is least willing**. Today that is him. **He is aware this will look, afterwards, like courage. It is not. It is that he cannot make his feet do the other thing.** `prowess >= 12`, and stubborn/cynical. Neither desc may name a place, have a rider arrive or a message go out, or count in thousands. The first may not name the faith, the god, the site, the rite, the building or the people whose place it is; nobody may pray, kneel, curse or invoke anything, and no priest, septon, godsworn or holy man may appear. The second may not name his faith, name a god, quote a prayer, name the site, name his own house or be seen to pray; nobody may bless him, notice him or say anything to him; and he may not describe what the place is for | 2 templates, base 50, 80 |

Batch 130 opened the marriage axis, read the two things AGOT hides inside it, and stopped there — so everything a man's household contains that is **not** his marriage has been invisible since plan 1, in a setting with salt wives, paramours and a dynasty that practised the arrangement for three hundred years. **The pair is disjoint by construction and the `count = 0` row is the reason.** The first is a man who has one now; the second is a man who does not have one now and had one before, and the second says so in its own body rather than relying on the first not firing. So he does not send the reserve at the moment when sending the reserve would feel best, and does not take the wing that would make the finer story and cost him forty horse he has no way of replacing. **Years of quietly keeping a household larger than the one people count when they count his leave neither generosity nor meanness, but a permanent low awareness of how far a given quantity has to reach** — and it reaches, today, precisely as far as he chooses to make it reach. The knight half is `bloodied`/`down` and takes `bc_bloodied`. **There was, in his life, a second set of rooms** — somewhere with its own hours and its own small settled routine and its own manner of asking what sort of day it had been. His eyes are ordinary and getting worse. The knight half is `wild`/`down` and takes `bc_spent_horse`. He puts the horse at it, gets four good strides out of the animal, and then it is screaming and going over on the near side and he is on his feet in the worst ground on the field, **doing exactly what he was told to do, at the price that had already been allowed for. Nobody will come and say a word to him about it later. From where the sum was worked there is nothing that needs saying.** `prowess >= 12`, and trusting/paranoid. Neither desc may name a place, have a rider arrive or a message go out, or count in thousands. Not cynicism — closer to bookkeeping, and it has predicted nearly every man he ever dealt with, because nearly every man does the thing that keeps what he has got. **The one directing the far line has more to lose today than anybody standing on this slope, and every scrap of it came from here. The knight half is `ordered`/`up` and takes `bc_pursuit`. **Most of the men in this rank are here because somebody they answer to is here, and if you traced that back far enough you would come to an actual reason, and not one of them has ever traced it. He counted the days off on his fingers in the yard before he rode out. **It is the least rousing reason to be standing on a field that has ever been thought of, and it is also the reason still standing after the rousing ones have burned themselves out somewhere in the middle of the day.** The far line goes and the whole front opens up, and the men either side of him are already shouting and moving badly, **and he goes forward at a speed the rest of them can actually hold, keeping his own dressing, because that is what going forward means and the other thing is only running.** Nobody sees him do it. The knight half is `entrenched`/`down` and takes `bc_shieldwall`. **Nine parts of this line would be entirely within their rights to look at what is coming and think of it as somebody else's quarrel. So he sets his feet and stays, **and the reason he stays has no nobility in it whatsoever: there is no direction he could move in that would still have the arrangement in it that he has spent his whole adult life standing inside.** The shields on his left begin to give and he does not go back with them. **What keeps him there is not courage and certainly not love — it is the flat fact that the voice off to the right is the one that has always told him where to be, and it has not yet told him anything else.** `prowess >= 12`, and brave/craven. | how many seats each of them actually holds | `any_held_title`, `has_primary_title`, `any_directly_owned_province` and `any_sub_realm_county` all returned zero across the whole mod. The mod had read **rank** eight times — `highest_held_title_tier` on this man or the man opposite, `is_powerful_vassal`, `is_independent_ruler` — and had never once read **how much**. Rank is the tallest thing a man holds and says nothing about whether he holds one of them or nine, which in this setting is most of the difference between a duke and a great house. **The syntax is vanilla's verbatim**: `gruesome_festival.txt:443-445` writes `any_held_title = { count >= 2  tier = tier_county }`, the count-plus-filter form, and `07_ep3_wars.txt:3970-3971` writes `every_held_title = { title_tier >= duchy }`, the comparison vocabulary — note these are two different vocabularies for the same fact, and the mod's existing rank reads use a third, the `highest_held_title_tier >= tier_kingdom` script-value form. All three are live in shipped files. **The pair is disjoint by construction and the `count = 0` row is the reason**, exactly as in batch 139: without it the second gate is a strict *superset* of the first — a man holding two duchies also holds something of county tier or better — and the first collapses into a subset of the second, which is the collision batch 72 shipped by accident. No guards; both are iterators over the scoped character's own held titles. **This is not the rank rows.** `bc_facing_a_crown_trigger` reads the *far* commander, and more importantly rank and quantity come apart in both directions: a vassal king holds one title of kingdom tier and may hold nothing else worth the name, while a man with four duchies and no crown outranks nobody and outweighs almost everybody. The second gate in particular passes men the rank reads cannot tell apart from a landless household knight. **It is not the weight rows either** — `is_powerful_vassal` is defined relative to a specific liege's realm, an independent duke is not one however much he holds, and a powerful vassal may hold exactly one duchy. The commander half is `adapted`/`down` and falls through the verb table. **A man with one seat behind him is fighting for one thing and can hold that one thing whole in his head while he works. He cannot.** What stands behind him is not a thing, it is a set, and every part of the set has its own hall and its own steward and its own quiet men who will hear about this afternoon at different speeds and draw quite different conclusions about what it means for them. **That is what he has been doing all day instead of the simpler work — not commanding so much as running each part of it forward in his mind and watching what today does to it there.** Twice now he has taken longer over a decision than the moment allowed, **because the answer was obvious for one part of what he holds and plainly wrong for another, and while he was reconciling the two the moment went by and took something with it**. There is a version of him that could have fought this the way the smaller men are fighting it, with nothing in his head but the ground immediately in front, and that version would be having a better afternoon. **He can see that version quite clearly. He cannot get inside it.** `stewardship >= 12`, and arrogant/patient. The knight half is `wild`/`up` and takes `bc_spent_horse`. **He is not one of the household men and he is not one of the great ones, and there is no restful place to stand between those two.** The household men have nothing and are owed everything; the great ones have everything and are owed nothing. He has a roof that is his, and the ground under it, and just about enough coming off that ground to keep one good animal and the harness to put on it — **and the whole condition of a man in his position is that everyone can see the size of what he holds and can therefore judge, to a nicety, whether he is carrying on as though he deserved more of it.** So when the far line starts to buckle he does not wait to be sent, **and he does not go at the pace that keeps a horse alive for a second day, and he does not go at the pace a sensible man would choose either.** The animal, which cost him a full year of what that ground makes, is being ruined underneath him while he does it, and he knows it, and it is the cheapest thing he will pay for today. **It works loudly, in front of precisely the people who needed to see it work, which is the whole reason he did it in that particular manner, and he will not admit that to a living soul.** `prowess >= 12`, and ambitious/content. Neither desc may name a place, have a rider arrive or a message go out, or count in thousands. The first may not name a seat, a title, a house, an heir or a wife, give a total for what he holds, or have anybody bring him word. The second may not name his holding, a title or a house, give a figure for his acres, his rents or his men, or speak to anybody | 2 templates, base 50, 80 |
| what the name is worth | The engine keeps a number for what a house is worth to people who have no history with it, and the mod had never read it, which the house-size batch's own header admits. A threshold and a ranking are not subsets of each other in either direction, because a man with diplomacy 8 can have diplomacy as his best skill while a man with diplomacy 16 can have martial 18 and fail the same gate. `highest_skill` excludes prowess and `highest_skill_including_prowess` does not, which is the only difference between them and the one that matters here - vanilla's `steal_herd_outcome_events.txt:295` passes `prowess` to the including form, and AGOT branches five ways on the excluding form at `yearly_events_7.txt:3739-3759` and picks a character's idle portrait animation off it at `b_agot_animations.txt:53`. A hundred and seventy-seven batches have read who is sworn to whom and none of them has read the written rule all of that runs under. AGOT ships its own `00_realm_laws.txt` and keeps the vanilla group: `crown_authority` is declared at line 22 with `cumulative = yes` and `default = crown_authority_1`, and the four levels sit at lines 29, 56, 144 and 238. The levels are named rather than asked for as a group maximum, because the dump says nothing about what `realm_law_group_at_maximum_level` returns for a character who holds no law in that group at all - a tribal ruler runs `tribal_authority` and a landless man runs nothing - while `has_realm_law` is simply false when the man does not hold that law.
