| the boy who used to knock him down in the yard | `has_relation_bully` and `has_relation_victim` were both at zero across the whole mod. The mod has read what these men are to each other from nearly every angle it can - blood, marriage, fostering, oath, wages, hooks, dread, opinion, alliance, homage - and never once read the oldest and least dignified of them, which is which of the two of them used to get hit. Live in both trees in the character-target form: vanilla writes both keys against `$CHARACTER$` through `00_relation_triggers.txt` from `:125`, negates `has_relation_victim = root` at `00_childhood_triggers.txt:78` and reads `has_relation_bully = root` in `00_character_descriptions.txt:877`, and AGOT writes the key in files of its own making at `agot_lmf_army_events.txt:386` and `:2376` and `agot_ub_on_actions.txt:45`. **This is not the fostering rows.** A fostering is an arrangement two grown men made about a boy and is the same fact read from both ends; this is what happened inside somebody's arrangement while nobody was writing it down, between two boys who were not consulted, and it points at whichever of them was smaller that year rather than at whichever was owed something. **Nor is it the rival, nemesis or grudge rows, and the engine says so rather than the comment** - `00_scripted_relations.txt:142` gives `bully` the line `corresponding = victim` and lists `rival`, `nemesis`, `grudge`, `friend` and `best_friend` among its `opposites`, so the man who is the scoped man's bully is by definition not also his rival. A rivalry is a thing two adults arrived at together; this one was arrived at by one of them. Each gate carries the negation of the other, in the fostering pair's arrangement rather than by argument. Commander half is `wild`/`down` and takes `bc_spent_horse`: he knows exactly what the fear is worth, which is nothing, and none of that has the slightest effect on the part of him that was nine and is quicker than he is, so it all goes out hot and he never once asks what the far side wants of him because he is not fighting the far side at all. Knight half is `measured`/`up` and falls through - he did not know the far side's hand, he knew what it was frightened of, and doing this extremely well while taking no pleasure in it is the only apology available to a man out here, and it is not one. Rarity: the relation is handed out by childhood events between two boys of close age in one hall, most men never acquire it in either direction, and then both have to live thirty years and end up on opposite sides of one war with one of them giving the orders. | 2 templates, base 50, 80 |
| the oath that had no lord in it | `has_relation_blood_brother` returned zero across the whole mod - the two hits on the word are a note about where a memory's shape was copied from and a line in the relation-count batch's header observing that `blood_brother` carries `relation_aliases = { friend }`. **One of the live rows is in this mod's own genre**: vanilla writes `has_relation_blood_brother = liege` inside a knight phase event, at `00_knight_phase_events.txt:1359`, as one arm of the test for which knights the severed-head content may pick, beside `is_acclaimed`, `is_close_family_of` and `is_powerful_vassal_of` - which is the company the engine itself thinks this relation keeps. It also writes it at `00_alliance.txt:399` and four times in `09_mpo_interactions.txt` from `:3043`, and AGOT ships twenty-two files carrying the key. **This is not the kin rows.** `is_close_family_of` is blood and marriage; blood-brotherhood is the arrangement men make precisely because they are not kin and cannot become kin. A brother is an accident; this was a decision, taken once, by two people, with nobody's permission. Nor is it the rival, nemesis or grudge rows or batch 200's pair, and the engine says so rather than the comment - `00_scripted_relations.txt:310` lists `rival`, `nemesis`, `grudge`, `bully`, `victim`, `potential_friend` and `potential_rival` among its `opposites`. Nor is it the relation counts, despite the alias: that gate asks how many standing bonds a man holds anywhere in the world, and this asks which man one particular bond points at, which is the distinction that batch's own header draws in the other direction. **The relation is symmetric, which is why the pair is two men and not two directions** - `corresponding = blood_brother` means there is no far end to read differently, so the fostering pair's construction is unavailable and the pair asks one question of two different men instead, in batch 149's arrangement. The second is knight-only by construction, which is batch 141's lesson: no man is his own blood brother, so the gate would be a silently never-firing row in the commander pool. Commander half is `entrenched`/`down` and takes `bc_shieldwall` - he puts himself at the worst of the bend and goes on standing there long after the arithmetic has stopped agreeing with him, because of all the men alive who might watch him give ground there is exactly one whose watching he cannot have, and the position holds longer than it has any right to and costs more than it is worth, which are two ways of saying the same thing. Knight half is `ordered`/`up` and takes `bc_pursuit` - everything in him wants to be first through the gap, and he goes instead at the pace the men either side can actually hold, because an oath between equals is a promise to be useful to a man rather than impressive at him. Rarity: the relation is granted by a single interaction between two grown men who already like each other enough to bleed on it, most men never swear it at all, and then the oath has to outlive whatever put the two of them on a field together. | 2 templates, base 50, 80 |
| the war that is all on one man's ledger | `war_contribution` returned zero across the whole mod, and the word "contribution" appeared exactly once and only as prose. The mod already reads the war behind the combat from several angles - how long it has run, whether it is a civil war, who leads it, which side of it this man is on, whether he has a second one at home - but never once reads the war's own ledger of who has actually done the work. Live in both trees on the war scope: AGOT uses it in a file of its own authorship at `agot_war_on_actions.txt:700`, `:721` and `:744` to find the three attackers with the most invested in a war about to be settled over their heads, and its bar for a man who has put something in is `value >= 10`; vanilla uses the same trigger at `00_war_effects.txt:2532` with `value > 0`, at `03_dlc_fp2_scripted_effects.txt:1259` against a stored debt, and at `07_dlc_ep3_scripted_effects.txt:11358`. Both gates ask for `value >= 100`, ten times AGOT's own bar. **The two gates are disjoint by construction**, in the fostering pair's arrangement: the first wants root to have carried this war and the far commander not to have, the second wants the reverse, and no war can satisfy both. Commander half is `adapted`/`down` and falls through: he paid for all of it out of one purse, the others came when they came, and when the ground goes wrong he gives up the shape he was holding for a worse one that will last longer, which works, and works less well than he needed. What sits badly is not the losing but that there is nobody to divide it with. Knight half is `entrenched`/`up` and takes `bc_shieldwall` - the man opposite put every part of that far rank there himself, so the knight does not hurry the opening, he plants his feet and offers nothing to work with and lets the weight of a war one man has been carrying alone settle further onto that one man. Rarity: war contribution accrues to whoever wins the battles and takes the holdings, which in most wars is one or two people out of a dozen participants, and a hundred of it is a man who has fought the war more or less by himself. | 2 templates, base 50, 80 |
| how many names are actually in this war | `any_war_participant`, `every_war_participant`, `any_war_attacker` and `any_war_defender` all returned zero across the whole mod. The war-scope gates already shipped ask how long the war has run, whether it is a civil war, who leads it, which side of it a man is on and now how much of it he has paid for - every one of them a question about one man or one war-wide flag, never about the size of the guest list. Live in both trees: AGOT uses `any_war_participant` in files of its own authorship in at least six places including `agot_war_on_actions.txt:345` and `:353`, `00_agot_mega_wars_effects.txt:1304` and `agot_scenario_roberts_rebellion_minor_events.txt:1001`, and the `count` form is live in vanilla on both lists - `hold_court_events_general.txt:8223` writes `any_war_participant = { count = 0 ... }` inside `vassal_war_conflict_trigger` and `09_dlc_mpo_scripted_effects.txt:4053` writes `any_war_attacker = { count >= 2 ... }`. **This is not the battle-size rows.** Those count men standing on this field; a war participant is a name with a stake in the war, and the two numbers move independently in both directions - one great lord can field fifteen thousand men with nobody else named in the war at all, and eight petty lords can be named in a war and between them field a fraction of that. The two gates are disjoint by construction, at least eight names against at most two, and two is the floor because a war has an attacker and a defender or it is not a war. Commander half is `measured`/`down` and falls through: he does the sum properly and the sum says what it said an hour ago, and the difficulty is not being wrong but that a war with this many names on it contains nobody who can be handed an unwelcome answer and simply take it. Knight half is `wild`/`up` and takes `bc_spent_horse` - nobody came, so there is no wider account to protect and no third party's timetable to keep, and he spends wind and horse and the good arm all at once because a quarrel small enough for two names is small enough to be settled for good. Rarity: most wars run to a leader, his opponent and whatever allies and called vassals either can raise, which lands well short of eight; at most two is rarer still, because it means one man declared on another and not one other person in the world took a side. | 2 templates, base 50, 80 |
| the ground was already somebody's problem | `is_occupied` and `is_raided` both returned zero across the whole mod, and so did `is_county_capital`, `has_ongoing_construction`, `num_buildings` and `available_loot`. The mod reads this province from several angles already - the six terrain bands, `fort_level`, `has_holding_type`, `has_special_building`, and through the county its development and its holder - and every one of those asks what the ground is or who holds it. **Not one of them asks what has been happening to it.** Neither gate mentions a terrain, so both live in `bc_scripted_triggers.txt` and not in the closed six-band file. **AGOT pairs these two keys itself**: `00_agot_colonization_decisions.txt:30` and `:31` are consecutive lines inside one `root.location = { }` block reading `is_occupied = no` and `is_raided = no` together, in a file of AGOT's own making, and AGOT reads `is_occupied = yes` again on a saved province scope at `agot_lmf_heir_events.txt:7488`, `:8608` and `:8942`. Vanilla has both: `ep3_decisions.txt:322` and `tgp_mandala_scripted_effects.txt` from `:3002` for the first, `mpo_migration_events.txt` from `:1490` for the second. A province can be occupied and raided at once, so each gate carries the negation of the other and the overlap belongs to neither. Commander half is `ordered`/`down` and falls through: the holding behind him changed hands before he arrived, so he does it by the book instead, forms clean and gives each instruction to the man it belongs to, and the whole thing goes off like a drill and none of it helps, because doing it properly on ground that is not yours is a very tidy way to lose. Knight half is `bloodied`/`up` and takes `bc_bloodied` - smoke in the wrong direction, riders working the same county at their leisure, and the thing he can beat is in front of him while the thing that will cost this county its winter is four miles off and has never once been inside his reach. Rarity: occupation needs somebody to have taken the holding and still be sitting on it, which is a minority of provinces even in wartime and a smaller minority of the ones a field battle happens in; an active raid is rarer still and does not last long. | 2 templates, base 50, 80 |
| how much of a place this place is | `is_county_capital`, `num_buildings`, `combined_building_level`, `building_slots`, `free_building_slots` and `has_free_building_slot` all returned zero across the whole mod. The occupation rows asked what has been happening to the ground; this asks how much is on it, which is a different fact - a burnt-out county seat has been happening to for months and is still the seat, and a quiet hillside can have nothing on it at all. **It is not the fortification rows either**: `fort_level` counts walls, which `num_buildings` does not count, and a great fortress can stand in a province carrying one economic building. Both keys are AGOT-original: `num_buildings` is read as `any_county_province = { num_buildings >= 2 }` at `00_agot_war_values.txt:94`, as `num_buildings > 1` at `00_agot_dragon_filler_events.txt:7802` and `:7836`, and as an `order_by` at `00_agot_nights_watch_wars.txt:282`, and `is_county_capital = yes` runs throughout `00_agot_standard_economy_buildings.txt` from `:710`. Vanilla has both too - `00_standard_economy_buildings.txt:3917` and `:3926` compare `num_buildings < 5` and `>= 8`, and the same file reads `is_county_capital` in both directions from `:77`. **The thresholds come from those files**: AGOT's own bar for a province anybody has built on is two and vanilla's own bar for a thin one is `< 5`, so the first gate asks for five or more, which is vanilla's line read from the other side, and the second asks for none, which is the floor. Disjoint by construction on both clauses. Commander half is `entrenched`/`down` and takes `bc_shieldwall`: a mill, a bridge, a row of thatch, and a dog going frantic behind a wall close enough to hear, so he sets his feet on the near edge of the road and will not give it up long past the point where the road matters, because giving it up means the next hour happens on the far side of that wall. Knight half is `adapted`/`up` and falls through - no roofline to judge a distance against and no lane to tell him which way a horse will go, so he stops using the country and starts using the men, and being un-clever in precisely the right way at precisely the right speed turns out to be the one thing this ground pays for. Rarity: a county has one capital and several provinces, so most ground is not the seat before the building count is asked at all, and nothing built at all is the other end and not the usual case either. | 2 templates, base 50, 80 |
| the fight already standing on this ground | `any_army_in_location`, `is_army_in_siege` and `army_owner` returned zero across the whole mod, and `is_at_war_with` appeared three times, every one of them the `is_at_war_with_liege` batch. The word "siege" turned up sixteen times and not one was a gate - the note that `is_under_siege` is absent from the 1.19.0.6 dump, the `siege_phase_time` ceiling read off a man's own trait XP, and the rest prose. Batch 121 counts the armies a **man** has raised, batch 190 reads the one army standing **under him**, batch 204 asks whether this ground has already been taken; **nothing had ever asked what other host is standing in this province and what it is doing here.** These two gates are two halves of AGOT's own script put together: `00_agot_lmf_triggers.txt:3` is a trigger of AGOT's own making called `capital_province_being_besieged` whose entire body is `capital_province = { any_army_in_location = { army_owner = { is_at_war_with = root } } }`, and `00_agot_colonization_decisions.txt:38` writes `any_army_in_location = { is_army_in_siege = yes }` inside `root.location`, eight lines below the `is_occupied`/`is_raided` pair the previous rows took from the same block. **The key counts interrupted sieges by design** - triggers.log defines `is_army_in_siege` as "in a siege (this includes a siege interrupted by combat)" - so a field battle in a province with a siege standing is nearly always a relief force arriving at the wall, and the besieging host is one of the two armies in this very combat. The first gate is that afternoon from the relief's side, the second is the same afternoon from the wall, and the second carries the first's negation. **Both read `scope:combat_side.side_commander` and not `root`, deliberately**: the knight who fires the second is a household man in nobody's war of his own, so `is_at_war_with = root` would come back false for every besieger alive and the gate would fire for very nearly everybody. It is not the fortification rows - `fort_level` asks whether there are walls here, which is true of a province standing empty in peacetime, while these ask whether anybody is at them today - and it is not the occupation rows, because a siege in progress is precisely a holding that has **not** fallen yet. Commander half is `bloodied`/`down` and takes `bc_bloodied`: the works, the stakes, the ditch, a camp that has had time to become a proper thing, and he goes in regardless because there is no version of this where he waits another week, bleeding into ground somebody else prepared while he keeps his face arranged in case the small figures on the parapet can make it out. Knight half is `ordered`/`up` and takes `bc_pursuit` - he is the one who bothered with the ditch on the quiet days, so he holds his length of line, does not step forward, does not let the man beside him step forward, and the far side arrives tired and uphill and out of order. Rarity: most battles in Westeros are fought on a road or a ford with nothing under siege for fifty miles, and then the commander has to be losing the fight to lift it or the knight winning the fight to hold it. | 2 templates, base 50, 80 |
| the same way of making war | `has_same_culture_martial_tradition`, `has_same_culture_language`, `has_same_culture_heritage` and `has_same_culture_ethos` all returned zero across the whole mod. Culture is read in nine live places and every one is one of three questions: whether the far commander shares his culture outright, whether **one** culture holds **one named** thing (`has_cultural_tradition`, `has_cultural_pillar`, `agot_is_wildling_culture`, `agot_is_westerosi_culture`), or how the two peoples **feel** about each other (batch 150's `cultural_acceptance`). **Nothing had ever asked whether two different peoples happen to make war the same way.** The key and its form are vanilla's and AGOT ships the same file: `10_culture_conversion_decisions.txt:74` writes `capital_province.culture = { has_same_culture_martial_tradition = root.culture }`, which is the dotted-target form both gates use, and AGOT carries that decision with the same line at `:90`; `culture_notification_events.txt:105` and `:117` put the key beside its three sisters in a `calc_true_if`, and `iberia_north_africa_events.txt:1667` writes it negated, which is what the second gate does. Both sides are named through `scope:combat_side` rather than `root`, which is batch 150's construction off the same two culture scopes. **Both gates require the two cultures to differ, and that clause is not decoration**: two men of one culture necessarily share a martial tradition, so without it the first gate would be a strict superset of `bc_foe_is_countryman_trigger` and would answer yes every time a lord met his own neighbour. It is not the named-custom rows either - those ask whether one culture holds one tradition or which ethos pillar it was built with, while this is a comparison on a different pillar, and two peoples can share it while holding no named tradition in common. Commander half is `measured`/`down` and falls through: he recognises the wheel they just did on the left because he was taught it at fourteen by a man with a stick, they are working from one book, and every clever thing he has left is a thing the far side already has the answer to. Knight half is `wild`/`up` and takes `bc_spent_horse` - nothing he was drilled against is on the field, so he gives up on being correct about it and goes forward on the theory that whatever they are doing still requires them to be somewhere. Rarity: most wars in Westeros put the same culture on both sides of the field, and inside that minority the first wants the two to have arrived at the same way of making war regardless and the second wants them not to have. | 2 templates, base 50, 80 |
| what arm the column is made of | `army_number_maa_regiments` returned zero across the whole mod, and so did `base_type`, `is_unit_type`, `heavy_cavalry`, `crossbow` and `bowmen`; `archers` had one hit and it was the English word in a comment. Two hundred and seven batches had counted the men in a host, its regiments, its rank, its ground, its weather, its stores and its errand, and never once asked what **arm** was standing in it. The header says plainly what the licence is: every live use of `army_number_maa_regiments_of_base_type` in both trees — vanilla's own `00_knight_phase_events.txt:1532` onward, six base types in a row — sits inside a `factor =` or a `value =` and is read as a **number**, not asked as a question, and two greps for a comparator after the key came back empty. The comparator form is licensed by the 1.19.0.6 dump alone, which gives the key `Traits: <, <=, =, !=, >, >=` on army scope, the same six it gives `army_supply`. Not batch 129, which counted regiments on a **man's books** and reads the same in his hall with no war on; not batch 188, which asked what **types** stand anywhere on the side and named only engines and the house guard; not batch 190, which walks to this same army and reads its **larder and its errand**. Disjoint by construction: two or more heavy horse and no archers at all against two or more archers and no heavy horse at all, each denying exactly what the other demands. Losing is `wild`/`down` — the most expensive thing he owns spent across open ground because he bought nothing that reaches. Winning is `entrenched`/`up` and `bc_shieldwall`, a foot line that holds the ground its bowmen need | 2 templates, base 50, 80 |
| whose chair is on the table | `is_contested` returned zero across the whole mod, as did the word "contested", `target_title` and the phrase "war goal". The mod had read the war behind the combat many times over — whether it is a rising, whether it is holy, who declared it, how long it has run — and it had read what men **hold**, in batch 143's seat-counting gates. It had never asked whether any of what they hold is the thing somebody is **suing for**. The header is careful about what the key actually says: not "is this title the goal of this war" but "is this title contested in **any** war", so a man can be fighting a border squabble this morning and still have his own seat named in somebody else's declaration. Live in both trees — vanilla gates title revocation on `is_contested = no` at `00_interaction_triggers.txt:94`, reads it through `any_held_title` at `07_ep3_values.txt:624`, and the key carries a **trigger localization entry** of its own, which the game only writes for keys meant to be shown in a tooltip. The `primary_title` walk is optional — a commander can be a landless household man — so both gates take the full-chain `exists` as a sibling and repeat it in the `trigger_if` limit. Both seats can be on the table at once, so the second gate **denies the first in its own body** and the tie goes to the near man, batch 121's construction. Losing is `ordered`/`down`: a formation that goes backwards without coming apart, which is also what he is doing to himself. Winning is `measured`/`up`, a man who turns down three chances to be magnificent because magnificent is how you hand a desperate opponent something to work with | 2 templates, base 50, 80 |
| how many banners are standing here | `side_participant` returned zero across the whole mod. `side_primary_participant` returns twenty-nine — it is how nearly every far-side gate reaches the man opposite — and not one of those twenty-nine asks how many participants there **are**. Two hundred and nine batches had counted the men in a host over and over and never once counted the **banners**. Not batch 136, which reads `any_war_ally` off each side's primary participant: that is a fact about the **war** — who signed what, who owes whom — and is true in a throne room with nobody in a field at all. The two come apart both ways: a man with six allies in the war can be on this field entirely alone, and a side of five participants can contain no allies whatever because every one of them was summoned and had no say. Not the size rows either, which count **men**. The form is shipped in a file of exactly this kind — vanilla writes `scope:combat_side = { any_side_participant = { ... } }` in `00_knight_phase_events.txt:1380` and AGOT ships the same block at `:1542` of its own copy. Each gate reads **both** sides, because a crowd is only interesting against something: four or more here and exactly one opposite, against exactly one here and four or more opposite, which is disjoint by construction twice over. Losing is `adapted`/`down` — four proud men who each hear the same simple order in a different shape, beaten by a thing with one head that turns faster. Winning is `bloodied`/`up`, a lone house working the seam between two lots who are each being careful with the other | 2 templates, base 50, 80 |
| how many ways in there are | `any_neighboring_province` returned zero across the whole mod, and so did "neighboring", "neighbouring", "crossroads" and `border_province`. Two hundred and ten batches have asked what the ground under the battle *is* - which of the six terrain bands, whether a river runs across it, whether the sea is at one edge, what is built on it, who holds it, how firmly - and not one has asked what it is *connected* to. Every ground row so far treats the province as an island with properties; none looked at the roads. Both gates carry vanilla's own `exists = county` filter, written that way at `hike.txt:74` and three times in `pilgrimage_events_seasia.txt`, and AGOT ships the same test at `ep3_laamps_provisions.txt:1068`. The filter is load-bearing rather than decorative: it throws away sea and impassable neighbours, the two kinds this mod cannot verify without a game running, and leaves a count of settled country next door. No live script in either tree pairs `count` with this iterator, so the pairing leans on the mod's own standing idiom instead. Five or more settled neighbours against one or fewer - three counties of daylight, and no province can answer yes to both. | 2 templates, base 50, 80 |
| what the war is actually for | `using_cb` returned zero across the whole mod; `casus_belli` appears seventeen times and every one is a citation inside a comment. The mod has read the war six ways - how long it has run, whether it is a rebellion, who leads it, which side a man is on, whether he has a second one at home, whose ledger paid for it - and never once asked what it is *about*. Vanilla stacks five `using_cb` inside an `OR` on a war scope at `00_alliance.txt:893-899`; AGOT writes a `NOR` of fourteen on `scope:war` at `agot_esr_on_actions.txt:4529-4545` and two more `OR` blocks in `agot_war_on_actions.txt`. Every key used here is an AGOT definition: the five pirate CBs from `00_agot_pirate_wars.txt` and `free_slaves_cb` / `slavery_revolt` from `00_agot_slavery_wars.txt`. This is not the `is_civil_war` gate - that asks whether the quarrel is inside one realm, these ask what it is about. The one honest overlap is `slavery_revolt`, which carries `group = civil_war`; `free_slaves_cb` carries `group = slavery` and is declared by an outside ruler, so the gate is neither subset nor superset. A war carries exactly one casus belli and no key is in both lists. | 2 templates, base 50, 80 |
| what condition the gear is in | `artifact_durability` returned zero across the whole mod, along with `artifact_max_durability`, `is_equipped`, `artifact_type`, `is_unique` and `has_artifact_modifier`. Outside comments the mod's only artifact reads are the Valyrian steel pair, which asks what a man's gear is *made of*, and the claim pair, which asks what gear he is *owed* and cannot reach. Neither asks what state the thing in his hand is actually in. `num_artifact_kills` was the first candidate on this scope and is the better story, but it has zero live usages in vanilla and zero in AGOT, so it was dropped. The bar is the engine's own `ARTIFACT_VERY_LOW_DURABILITY = 5.0`, identical in both trees' `00_defines.txt`; vanilla compares against it in `00_artifact_interactions.txt` and ships `on_artifact_durability_very_low`, and AGOT compares against it in `at_is_worthless_artifact_trigger`. The iterator is `any_equipped_character_artifact` for the Valyrian pair's reason - a sword in a vault at home is not on this field, and does not wear out in front of anybody either. Unlike that pair the two ends are made disjoint with a tie-break negation, because two men whose gear is five points off breaking is not a picture either moment wants to share. | 2 templates, base 50, 80 |
| how big the name he was given got | `accolade_rank` returned zero across the whole mod, and so did "accolade rank", `accolade_glory`, `accolade_type` and the bare word "glory". The mod already reads accolades twice - whether a man is somebody's acclaimed successor, and whether the commander has any acclaimed knights standing behind him - but both of those ask whether the thing **exists**. Neither asks how far it has **come** since it was handed over, which is the only question a man who has carried one for twenty years actually asks about it. The bar at the top is vanilla's own: `accolade = { accolade_rank >= 5 }`, written exactly that way in `00_clothing_triggers.txt:290-291` to decide which men are dressed as somebody, with a `>= 3` band two lines under it at `:298-299`. The bottom uses the same shape against `<= 1`, and vanilla reads the low end too - `>= 1` at `bp2_yearly_events_6.txt:13572`, `< 6` and `> 3` bands in `feast_default_events.txt`. The `accolade` link is optional on a character, so both gates take `exists = accolade` as a sibling and repeat it in the `trigger_if` limit, which is how vanilla writes it twelve times in `04_ep2_accolade_triggers.txt` from `:230`. A man holds at most one accolade, so rank five-and-up against rank one-and-under is disjoint by construction with three ranks of daylight between them. | 2 templates, base 50, 80 |
| how many men he holds by the throat | `any_hooked_character` returned zero across the whole mod, and so did `any_hooked_character_all`, `ordered_hooked_character`, `random_hooked_character`, `has_strong_hook` and `has_weak_hook`. The mod reads hooks exactly twice, and both of those gates are about **one rope between two named men** - whether the man whose war this is holds something over him, and whether he holds something over the man whose war this is. Neither has ever asked how many ropes he is holding altogether, which is a fact about a career rather than about a war. The form is AGOT's own: `agot_colonization_events.txt` writes `any_hooked_character = { ... count > 0 }` at `:666-670` with a literal count and walks straight into an `ordered_hooked_character` after it. Vanilla pairs the iterator with `count >=` four times in `tgp_mandala_triggers.txt`, and the key carries a **trigger localization entry** of its own, which the game only writes for keys meant to be read out in a tooltip. Three is the bar and the bar is load-bearing: `count >= 1` would be a strict superset of the shipped gate about his own war leader, and at three the two come apart both ways. Both commanders can be collectors, so the far gate **denies the near one in its own body** and the tie goes to the near man, batch 121's construction. Losing is `ordered`/`down` - a formation that keeps its shape going backwards, held by a man whose whole standing needs somebody willing to be shamed and cannot find one out here. Winning is `adapted`/`up`, a knight who works out that the men opposite are held rather than willing and stops leaning on the thick part of their line. | 2 templates, base 50, 80 |

**All six ground bands are now gated on as well as narrated.**
`bc_scripted_triggers.txt` wrapped four of six with a note to add the rest when
something needed them; `bc_the_broken_ground_holds` and `bc_the_green_closes_in`
are that something, and the wrapper set now matches `bc_ground_triggers.txt` one
for one.

**The two new foe triggers are not a ladder.** `bc_foe_is_countryman_trigger`
and `bc_foe_is_heathen_trigger` each test one property of the man opposite, so
unlike the arms of `bc_write_bond_effect` they are not mutually exclusive — a
foe can be both, and both templates can be valid on the same roll. The bond
*clause* still picks one, because that is a ladder; the *gates* are not.

**Every (KIND, SWING) pair the seam defines is now reached.** Plan 1 recorded
`entrenched`/`down`, `wild`/`up` and `ordered`/`down` as unreachable; later
content batches reached the first two, and this session's knight trait batch
reached the third. The pair census and the modifier reach counts in
`bc_ledger_effects.txt` are re-counted mechanically at the end of every batch —
plan 1's eighteen call sites are now **four hundred and fifty-five**, its "3, 3, 4 and 3"
are 76, 76, 75 and 38, and the fall-through set is one hundred and ninety sites rather than
five. The table's *behaviour* is unchanged; only its comments went wrong, and wrong in the
direction that invites a bad fix, which is why the counting command is now
written into the comment that carries the numbers.

**Template totals are now commander 233, knight 201.** Weight against vanilla's
`commander_none` at 1000: roughly 400 (the calibrated seven) + 300 + 180 + 70
+ 90 + 110 + 100 + 70 + 60 + 120 + 120 + 80 + 120 + 100 + 100 + 50 + 60 + 100 + 100 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50 + 50; on the knight side, two hundred and one templates against
`knight_none`'s 2000 — two hundred at 80 and `bc_knight_pulls_him_out` at 60,
held below the rest because it is the one knight moment that writes something
permanent.
If a sweep shows the original seven crowded out, **the character batch is the
first to cut** — it is the newest and the least measured.

**Pooling is `random_valid`, and it is vanilla in three separate shapes.**
`00_animal_custom_loc.txt` for a customizable_localization; `birth.9002`
(`events/birth_events.txt:3515-3614`) for a `random_valid` nested inside a
`first_valid` with a bare fallback below it, which is the pattern the eve now
uses and which only works because an empty pool is *not valid* and `first_valid`
moves past it; `chariot_race_ongoing_jp.0400` for bare untriggered `desc` lines
inside a pool. The memory types were already built this way and simply had
one-line pools.

**`validate` was wrong about pools and was fixed in the same session.** Its
arm-shape check demanded an unguarded `text` arm from every
customizable_localization definition including `random_valid` ones — but under
pooling an unguarded arm is valid on *every* render, so it competes with the
real prose and takes its share, and for `bc_bond_clause`'s empty-string fallback
that means deleting a clause that was available. The rule mandated a defect. It
is now suspended for pooled definitions, and the obligation moves to the mod,
which writes its fallback arm as a bare `trigger_if` — true when its limit is
false, so it is valid on exactly the absent and unknown-value paths.

## Not yet verified in game

- The shieldbrother memory rendering, and that `[shieldbrother.GetFullName]`
  resolves.
- The 2026-08-14 localization fixes: that
  `[bc_named_commander.GetNicknameNoTooltip]` resolves inside the award toast
  (including in the liege branch, where the scope has moved), and that the 26
  new `nick_agot_*_desc` keys render on hover in the character sheet with no
  `Duplicate localization key` line in `error.log`.
- The nickname award **under its real gate**. The path itself is proven (see the
  2026-08-14 run) but no commander has yet banked three notable wins with
  `martial >= 14` and no nickname unaided. `"BC: nickname awarded"` is a permanent
  marker, so `probe 'BC:'` will report it the first time it happens.
- **The nemesis relation itself, on a character screen.** `BC: nemesis new` fired
  32 times and `BC: nemesis rung grudge` 26, so `bc_rivalry_set_relation_effect`
  runs and picks the right rung — but a marker only proves the effect was
  *reached*, not that the relation *landed*. Plan 4 targets
  `scope:bc_rivalry_foe`, saved from `var:bc_nemesis`, on the strength of an
  argument about shipped syntax: `set_relation_X = var:Y` has **zero** uses in
  vanilla or AGOT, while `has_relation_X = var:Y` ships. That argument is
  untested. Open a commander who took a `BC: nemesis new` and look for a grudge
  against the right man with the reason *"beat him on the field"*. If the marker
  fired and no relation exists, the target form is wrong and must be re-derived.
- The rivalry game rule rendering: **Battle Chronicles: Rivalry** with three
  readable options on the rules screen.
- **Whether a `combat_side` survives a day.** The dead-knight read is now deferred
  into `bc_chronicles.0093`. `BC: dk deferred` counting while
  `BC: dk deferred fired` is absent means the side object was freed and the read
  must be re-hung on the commander character. Both counting with
  `BC: dk found dead` still 0 means a day is not enough, or knights are not dying.
- **The wound, at last measurably.** `"BC: wound"` has never fired, and until
  2026-08-15 that was starvation — it had one call site on an arm that resolved
  once per run. It now also hangs off the major arm (~45/run).
  `BC: wound gates passed` counting with `BC: wound` 0 means the
  `var:bc_battle_size >= 3` read rejects; both 0 while `BC: moment major` counts
  means the five-row outer limit does.
- **That the SWING row rejects.** It was inert until the major arm started feeding
  it wins. Roughly a third of what now reaches `bc_wound_effect` is `swing up`,
  and nothing has yet proven the row turns those away.
- **The decisive rate at the new floor.** `>= 4` scored 1 in 44 battles. The
  binding constraint is call-site rarity, not the floor — only 10 of 233 moments
  ever request `decisive` and 7 request it too early. Both numbers should be
  re-measured together, never one alone.
- **The spike fragment.** `bc_banner_falls_ground`, invoked as
  `[bc_chronicle_subject.Custom('bc_banner_falls_ground')]` from
  `bc_banner_falls_toast_desc`. The next run must show a Banner Falls toast
  carrying a clause that names the ground, and `probe 'BC: ground'` must report
  a band for the same battle. **It is no longer the last clause on that toast**
  and looking for it there is how this check gets read as failed: the desc runs
  literal, ground, bond, scale, and the two shared clauses were wired onto all
  ten toasts by Plan B task 4. Read the markers and the toast together:

  | markers | toast | verdict |
  |---|---|---|
  | a band | ground clause present, matches band | shape proven — Plan B tasks 5-14 unblocked. Tasks 2, 3 and 4 shipped without it, under the plan's AMENDMENT: a clause APPENDED to a desc that keeps its literal fails to "no improvement", while the core sets tasks 5-14 write REPLACE the literal and fail to an empty toast. |
  | a band | no ground clause | **not a mechanism failure** — vanilla ships this exact shape. `court_poet_poetry_desc` (`localization/english/court_positions_2_l_english.yml:150`) is `"[court_poet_from_task.Custom('GeneratePoem_Outro_Rhyming')]"`, rendered by the `send_interface_toast` at `common/court_positions/tasks/00_court_poet_tasks.txt:94-98` against a scope saved earlier in that same file (`00_court_poet_tasks.txt:47`) — a saved scope, a bare `Custom`, in a toast desc. Eleven other vanilla toasts do the same. So a blank clause means the **anchor** or the **variable**, not the mechanism. **The retry that used to be needed here is not:** `bc_bond_clause` now renders on the same toast off the same anchor, and `bc_write_bond_effect` writes on every path, so a bond sentence present with the ground clause absent isolates the **variable**, and all three clauses absent at once isolates the **anchor**. **The two inferences do not rest on the same thing and only one of them is fragile.** The bond-present one rests on `bc_write_bond_effect` writing bare on the caller's current scope, which is the anchor only because `bc_battle_open_effect` is its sole caller — the paragraph named `NOTE ON SCOPE` in `bc_context_effects.txt` says that much, and the rest is that effect's own opening line, an unconditional `save_scope_as = bc_chronicle_subject`, which makes its current scope the anchor on every path. Give that effect a second caller on any other scope and characters other than the commander start carrying `bc_battle_bond`, so a rendered bond clause stops ruling out the failure the row below describes — the anchor resolving to somebody who is not the commander — and therefore stops vouching for the anchor. The all-three-absent one is unaffected by a second caller — a second caller adds a write elsewhere, it does not remove the one `bc_battle_open_effect` already performs on the commander — and fails only if that effect itself stops running on the anchor. Only if all three are absent, retry with `bc_commander`, saved on the same character fifteen lines above the toast (`bc_commander_phase_events.txt:65`; the toast is at `:80-85`). Do not abandon the design on this symptom. |
  | a band | ground clause present, wrong band | the arms read the variable off the wrong character — the anchor resolves but not to the commander who holds the scratch. |
  | `BC: ground missing` | no ground clause | not a spike failure — `combat.location` did not resolve at a phase event. Fix the read site in `bc_write_ground_effect` and run again. |
- **The widened eve.** `bc_chronicles.0011` now assembles three paragraphs and
  can offer eleven options — three unconditional and eight gated. Nothing static
  proves a `first_valid` picks the arm intended, or that an `option` `trigger`
  hides rather than greys.

  | markers | screen | verdict |
  |---|---|---|
  | `BC: ground <band>` (write-path marker, not the eve's own read) | a third paragraph naming that band | the write path and the eve's `scope:bc_eve_ground` read agreed — both work. A mismatch between the write-path band and the rendered paragraph is itself informative: the scope saved the province but a subsequent battle overwrote `BC: ground <band>` while the scope still points to the first one. |
  | `BC: ground` for **any named band** — rough, forest, jungle, winter, desert | third paragraph reads "The field is what it is" | `scope:bc_eve_ground` is saved but the band triggers do not match through it — check the scope's type, not the triggers. This is a real failure: all five have named arms and none of them should reach the fallback. Desert is the one band the DIAG run's Q5 terrain census never produced (0/137), so an absent `BC: ground desert` proves nothing either way. |
  | `BC: eve ground missing` | third paragraph reads "The field is what it is" | correct behaviour: the ground scope never saved, the fallback renders as designed. Not a trigger bug. The insert may be in the wrong branch of `bc_eve_offer_effect` if this counts while `BC: eve offered` counts equally. |
  | `BC: ground open` or unresolved province | third paragraph reads "The field is what it is" | correct: open is the only band that legitimately rides the fallback, and an unresolved province also reaches it. |
  | any | option d visible on open ground | the `trigger` is not gating; every option in the mod is then suspect |
  | `BC: eve option ground` counts | — | the option is reachable and taken. There is no "offered but not taken" marker. |
- **The `bc_ground_held` modifier on a character screen.** It is the fifth
  family-2 modifier and the first added since the cleanup rule was written. The
  run must show it appear when option d is taken and be **gone** by the next
  battle of the same war. If it is still there, `bc_cleanup_effect` did not run
  or did not reach the new line.
- **The sixteen new templates, one marker each.** Every one writes
  `BC: fired <key>`, so `probe 'BC: fired'` is a census of which gates are
  reachable. A template at **zero** is a gate that never opens, and the three
  worth suspecting first are `bc_dust_and_thirst` (the desert band scored 0/137
  and 0/360 in two censuses — a statement about where these campaigns were
  fought, not about the map), `bc_the_wine_at_noon` (drunkard is rare on a man
  trusted with a host) and `bc_the_practised_eye` (`strategist` **and** a
  15,000-man host at once). A template that **dominates** is the other failure:
  the six character-batch gates are the widest in the mod and the batch header
  names them as the first weights to cut.
- **That the new knight cases are seen at all.** The file header records
  `base = 40` behind narrow gates producing **zero** fires across a session
  against `knight_none` at 2000. All six shipped knight additions sit at 80. If
  the second and third batches are at zero while the shipped three fire, the
  base is the reason and not the gates.
- **That a pooled `random_valid` inside the eve's `first_valid` falls through
  when empty.** This is the one structural claim of the prose work, and vanilla
  `birth.9002` is the whole of the evidence. The test is cheap: fight on winter
  ground and read the third paragraph. **Winter or desert text = the nesting
  works.** "The field is what it is" on a battle whose markers say winter means
  the empty-pool fallthrough does *not* work the way `birth.9002` implies, and
  every pooled arm has to go back to a flat `triggered_desc` chain.
- **That the pools actually vary.** Three eves in one game should not print the
  same foe paragraph three times, and a knight with several battle memories
  should not have them all read identically. Uniform output with the structure
  in place means `random_valid` is being read as `first_valid`.
- **The style-branched nickname ladder.** `BC: nickname style UNMATCHED` is a
  permanent marker and **must stay at zero** — it counts a `var:bc_style_last`
  holding a value none of the six ladders claims, which is the seam's KIND
  vocabulary and the ladder having drifted apart.
- **The foe-relative award.** `probe 'BC: award'` reports which of the two
  currencies paid. The three markers `piety` / `prestige` / `no creed` partition
  the notable wins that reached the award; `nemesis` is a subset of the first
  two. A run where `BC: award no creed` dominates means most notable wins carry
  no BC moment — not that the award is broken. Separate the two causes by
  crossing it against `BC: bond nofoe`, which counts only the leaderless-enemy
  case. `BC: award piety` at zero while `BC: creed heathen` counts is the real
  failure: the read is landing on the wrong character.
